  - **Bounds-check elimination:** `#no_bounds_check` only where the checker proves the index safe: `for each`, `repeat`, and `for i from 1 to count xs` while the list is not changed inside the loop (the index keeps that proof, like Goose's index types). Everywhere else the check stays, because an index out of range must show the overlay and give an empty value in both back ends (§5.15). `roci check --why-slow` (§5.3) also lists the lines that keep a check, as Goose's `--bce-lines` does.
  - **Nested data stored contiguously:** lists of same-shaped records as contiguous arrays or `#soa`, nested records stored in place (§5.2).
  - **Memory freed at scope end, partly:** values the checker proves never leave a frame go on the stack or in the frame arena (§5.14). Goose rejects a program where a reference could outlive its owner; rocinante cannot, because that would be a lifetime error users see (non-negotiables 2 and 4), so values that escape use the long-lived allocator.
  - **Messages copy flat values:** threads without shared memory is already the model of `send` (§4.5); compiled code copies a flat record in one copy.

  Not adopted: 1-, 2- and 4-byte relative links (they matter only for very large structures; reconsider if a benchmark shows memory traffic dominating, as the 184-byte commands did in Phase 1); variable-size enums and errors returned across frames (rocinante has neither, §5.2); saving and loading raw self-relative data (`save` and `load` go through formats, §6); and compiling and running in process with TinyCC (it is LGPL, §3, and iOS forbids running generated code).
- **Source mapping.** Crashes and profiler output in packaged sketches must point at `.roci` lines. Investigate whether Odin can carry line information from generated code; otherwise keep a map from generated Odin lines to `.roci` lines and translate reports.
- **iOS.** Released sketches are ordinary native code, so App Store Review Guideline 2.5.2 (no downloading or executing code that changes the app's functionality) is not an issue for shipped sketches. Nothing runs `.roci` source on the phone: iOS and Android are targets only (§7), so there is no interpreter on a device.

### 4.8 User interface (decided 2026-10-23)

UI elements are first-class citizens: built-in verbs, drawn by Dear ImGui through SDL3, identical on every platform.

- **Immediate mode.** Widgets are verbs called every frame inside a `ui [ ]` block; they return their current value (or `true` on the frame a button is clicked). No callbacks, no widget objects, no syncing between UI state and sketch state. This matches the paradigm in §5.2: widgets are verbs, values are plain data.
- **Engine: Dear ImGui** (MIT). Its official backends `imgui_impl_sdl3` (input, platform) and `imgui_impl_sdlgpu3` (rendering through the SDL3 GPU API) mean no custom renderer integration. UI is drawn last in each window's render pass, so it is exported, recorded and golden-image tested like any other drawing. It always draws on top of the canvas and ignores canvas transforms, cameras and styles. Users only ever see rocinante verbs and rocinante's theme; Dear ImGui never appears in the language, docs or errors.
- **Odin bindings** come from **dear_bindings** (MIT), which generates a C API (`dcimgui.h`) and a JSON description from which we generate Odin bindings. The SDL3 backend is in dear_bindings' tested list; `imgui_impl_sdlgpu3` is not yet, so verify its conversion in Phase 0 and contribute fixes upstream. Generated bindings live in `vendor/`.
- **Accessibility: our own AccessKit bridge** (decided 2026-10-23). Dear ImGui has no screen-reader support, so we build it. Every widget goes through a rocinante verb, so the core always knows each widget's role, label, value, state and focus, even though Dear ImGui doesn't keep them. Each frame, `ui` builds an AccessKit tree from its own widget records plus Dear ImGui's item rectangles and IDs, sends updates only when something changed, and turns AccessKit action requests (focus, press, set value, increment, decrement) into the same changes a mouse or keyboard would make. AccessKit (Apache-2.0 OR MIT; Chromium-derived parts under a BSD-style licence) is used through its C bindings, accesskit-c. Its release workflow publishes prebuilt libraries for macOS (arm64, x86_64), iOS (arm64 and the simulators), Windows (arm64, x86, x86_64), Linux (x86, x86_64) and Android (arm64-v8a, x86_64). It publishes none for FreeBSD, OpenBSD or arm64 Linux, although its Unix adapter is compiled for FreeBSD too. accesskit-c is written in Rust, so for those targets we build it once per release on the release machines (never on users' machines) and offer the targets upstream. AccessKit's adapters cover Windows (UI Automation), macOS (NSAccessibility), Unix (AT-SPI over D-Bus), Android and iOS. The Unix adapter works with Orca on Linux, FreeBSD and OpenBSD (checked by hand 2026-11-10, below). Offer the bridge upstream to Dear ImGui as a reusable piece. **Built so far** (2026-11-06, no adapter yet): `roci/ui/access.odin` makes the tree (`Node`s with role, name, value, state, the actions they take and their bounds) from each frame's records, nesting panels, tabs and menus, adding "controls hidden, press backtick to show" first while the interface is hidden, and giving it the focus, because a screen reader says what takes focus and does not announce a line that only appears; `actions.odin` queues a screen reader's requests (from any thread) and the widgets take them at the next frame (click, increment, decrement, set value, focus; choosing a radio button, list item or drop-down entry by text; selecting a tab; folding a panel), so a kept value and `ui.toml` change as they do for a mouse; menus cannot yet be opened by request. The trees of the examples are stored as text in `tests/ui-tree/`. **The macOS adapter** (2026-11-09, `roci/access.odin`, `access_darwin.odin`): after each frame the tree is hashed and sent to the system only when it changed; a screen reader's first request for the tree and every later update are served from a locked copy of the last tree, so AccessKit may ask from any thread; its actions are translated and queued on the interface (`ui.push_action`). Bounds are the widgets' rectangles in logical pixels from the window's top left. **The Unix adapter** (2026-11-09, `roci/access_unix.odin`, Linux, FreeBSD and OpenBSD): the same tree through `accesskit_unix_adapter` (AT-SPI over D-Bus; its callbacks come from its own threads), with the window's place on the screen (inner and outer boxes, from SDL; under Wayland the position is unknown and stays 0, 0) told to it at every update and after a move or a resize, and the window's focus. Written against the header and checked to compile for the three systems, and **tried with Orca on Linux, FreeBSD and OpenBSD (2026-11-10), where it works**: AT-SPI is there on all three (`at-spi2-core`, a D-Bus session bus, `speech-dispatcher` and a voice: `espeak-ng` on Linux and FreeBSD, `espeak` on OpenBSD, which has no `espeak-ng`; Orca is `orca` on Arch, `accessibility/orca` and `x11/gnome/orca`). The first runs found widgets whose changes Orca did not speak, because Orca speaks a change only from what it holds as the focus: the tree said nothing had focus on a radio button, a list entry, the steps of a number box or an open drop-down, and the slider and number box took the arrow keys as navigation (fixed 2026-11-11, below). Node bounds are in logical pixels, not device pixels, which only matters for a reader's mouse review on a scaled display. **The Windows adapter** (2026-11-11, `roci/access_windows.odin`): the same tree through AccessKit's subclassing adapter, which takes over the window's message handling, so it follows the window's focus by itself and answers UI Automation without the program handling a message. It has to be made before the window is first shown (and stops the program, a Rust panic across the C boundary, if the window is visible), and a window of a sketch is on screen from the moment it opens, so the attach hides a showing window and shows it again, which a person sees as one blink at the start; a cleaner order (the window made hidden and shown after its first frame) waits until the adapter has been tried. The bounds of the nodes are device pixels counted from the top left of the window, so the logical ones are multiplied by the window's density, and the tree is made again when a move or resize changes it. **Tried with Narrator and NVDA on 2026-11-11, where it works** (`examples/ui` on Windows 11, drawn with Direct3D 12): the names, roles and values, the arrow keys on the slider and the number box, the button, the check box, the radio buttons and the backtick. Reading and driving the tree through UI Automation from PowerShell first found that a radio button was marked selected (ARIA's `aria-selected`, for list items and tabs) where AccessKit takes a radio button's state from checked (`aria-checked`) on every system, so Windows offered no way to choose one; it is checked now. A drop-down cannot be opened by request (its node lists no expand action), like a menu.
- **Identity** (revised 2026-10-24). Each widget is identified by its label, its enclosing panel and tab path, and the loop index when it is made inside a loop (mapped to Dear ImGui IDs), so values survive hot reload and editing nearby code, and widgets made in a loop (mixer channels) stay apart. Two widgets with the same label and path in the same block are a load-time error with a friendly message.
- **Memory.** Widget values persist across hot reload and are saved in `ui.toml` in the sketch folder, which is committed to git (§4.9), keyed by the readable identity path above. `ui.toml` is written when the sketch exits and after a pause in changes, never on every slider move, so a performance does not leave the working tree dirty. `forget ui` resets them.
- **Text input and IME** use SDL3's text input API through the SDL3 backend: `SDL_StartTextInput` enables text and IME editing events and, on some platforms, shows the on-screen keyboard; `SDL_StartTextInputWithProperties` requests number fields and multi-line input; the IME window is placed with `SDL_SetTextInputArea`. Verify the backend's IME handling against non-Latin input in Phase 0.
- **Input model.** Mouse, touch, pen and keyboard all work. Dear ImGui's keyboard navigation is enabled by default: Tab and Shift+Tab move focus, Enter activates, Escape cancels. Tab never does anything else; hiding the UI has its own key (§5.8). Every widget is usable without a mouse. **The arrow keys move the value of a widget that has one** (decided 2026-11-11, as in every desktop toolkit and as a screen reader expects): while a slider, knob, range handle, pad or number box has focus, right and up add a step (a hundredth of the range, 1 for a number) and left and down take one away, and the focus stays where it is; elsewhere they move the focus as Dear ImGui does. A slider takes no typing (Dear ImGui turns a slider reached by Tab into a box of text, whose old number Return would put back); `number` is the widget for typing a number, and while it is typed in, up and down step the text. The number box's minus and plus are for the mouse and are no stops for Tab, and a click on one leaves the focus on the box, as a spin button does, so a screen reader speaks the new number. The item with focus in a radio group, a list or an open drop-down is the tree's focus, and an open drop-down shows its items. A range and a pad are each a group of two sliders for a screen reader, which has a role for one value in a range and none for two values or a place on a plane: a range's are its low and high ends (`size, low end`), each handle being a stop of its own, and a pad's are across and down (`end, across`), the focus being on the axis the arrows last moved. Touch arrives as mouse input in Dear ImGui; we enlarge hit targets on touch devices through the style scale and handle scrolling by drag ourselves.
- **HiDPI.** Everything scales with display density.
- **Theme.** One good default theme, light and dark, following the system setting, designed for rocinante rather than Dear ImGui's stock look. Customise with verbs (`ui theme dark`, `ui color accent #ff8800`, `ui font "Inter"`, `ui size 1.2`), never with style sheets; these map onto Dear ImGui's style settings internally. **`ui theme` is built** (2026-11-11): `ui theme dark`, `ui theme light` and `ui theme system` (the third, which the first plan did not have, is how a sketch goes back to following the computer, so a choice can be undone), a word checked against its list, kept for each window (`Win.ui_theme`) and applied every frame, so `system` follows a change of the computer's setting while the sketch runs; a window with no screen that follows the system is dark, which is why the golden images and stored trees are dark. Asked for by a Windows computer that could not be set to dark colors (Windows locks the setting until it is activated). The accent, font and size verbs are still to come.
- **Widgets Dear ImGui lacks** (`knob`, `xypad`, `range`, and a `colorpicker` in the form we want) are written with Dear ImGui's draw-list API inside `ui`, behaving exactly like the built-in ones: focus, keyboard, touch, theme and AccessKit roles included.
- **Version.** Pin one Dear ImGui version per rocinante release and upgrade deliberately, regenerating bindings each time.
- **Text in UI vs canvas.** Dear ImGui rasterises its own UI text; canvas text (`text "hello"`) uses SDL3_ttf (§4.3). Phase 3 must make both use the same default font so UI and canvas look like one system.

### 4.9 Sketch folders and assets (decided 2026-10-23)

A sketch that has files lives in a sketch folder. Everything it ships with is in `assets/`.

```
my-sketch/
  sketch.roci          # roci new starts it with: circle at 100 100 radius 50
  assets/
    images/  sounds/  videos/  fonts/  3d-models/  ml-models/  shaders/  data/
  output/              # saved frames, recordings, exports, build logs; ignored by git
  libs/                # vendored libraries from `use` (§5.10); committed to git
  icons/               # icon.svg or icon.png, and every system's icons `roci package` makes from it; committed to git
  ui.toml              # remembered UI values; committed to git
  memory.toml          # what `remember` keeps (§5.17); ignored by git unless committed on purpose as a starting state
  sketch.toml          # optional: packaging details and version; created by `roci package`; committed to git
  .gitignore
  .gitattributes       # only when Git LFS was chosen
```

- **A single file still works.** A lone `.roci` file with no folder runs (non-negotiable 1). `assets/` is where files go once a sketch has any, never a precondition.
- **What goes where.** `images/` (PNG, JPEG, …), `sounds/` (WAV, FLAC, MP3, Ogg), `videos/` (movie files for `movie`), `fonts/` (TTF, OTF, including copied system fonts, below), `3d-models/` (glTF, OBJ, SVG for 3D import, §6), `ml-models/` (LiteRT `.tflite` and `.litertlm` files, §4.10), `shaders/` (user shaders), `data/` (JSON, CSV, text). `roci add` picks the folder from the file type. Folder names are lowercase, like everything else in rocinante (§5.1).
- **Assets are found by name, not path.** `load "cat.png"`, `font "Inter"`, `sound "rain.wav"` search all of `assets/`, subfolders included. The subfolders are a tidy convention, not an address. If two files share a name, the checker says so and asks for the subfolder: `load "images/cat.png"`.
- **Bundled files come only from `assets/`.** A literal path outside it is a load-time error that teaches: "`/Users/…/cat.png` is outside your sketch, so it wouldn't travel with it. Copy it into `assets/`, or run `roci add /Users/…/cat.png` and I'll copy it for you." This is what makes sketches portable: a zip or a `git clone` runs anywhere, and iOS and Android (which can't read arbitrary paths) behave like the desktop.
- **The icon is one picture in `icons/`** (decided 2026-11-22): `icons/icon.svg` (a square view box) or `icons/icon.png` (square, 1024 pixels or more), or `roci package --icon file`, which checks it and copies it there. `roci package` makes every system's icons from it into `icons/`, in each system's shape (`cli/package_icons.odin`, the code map's packaging entries), and they are committed; a file there that `roci` did not make, or that changed after it made it (`icons/.made` keeps the sums), is the person's own and is never written over. `icons/` is not bundled: each package takes what its system needs. `sketch.toml`'s old `icon` key is said to be no longer read and taken out when the file is written.
- **Packaging bundles all of `assets/` and `libs/` (§5.10).** Computed names (`load ("frame" + n + ".png")`) can't be traced, so nothing is dropped. `roci package` lists files that no literal in the sketch mentions, as a hint, without removing them.
- **Live assets.** While `roci run` is going, a changed asset (repainted image, re-exported model, edited shader) reloads in the running sketch.
- **Runtime files are the deliberate exception.** Files chosen while the sketch runs may come from anywhere the platform allows: `ask file`, `ask folder`, dropped files, `files in "~/Pictures"`. They are never bundled, because they belong to whoever is running the sketch. On iOS and Android these go through the system file pickers.
- **Output.** `save`, `record` and exports write to `output/` by default, so renders never clutter the repository. An explicit path is allowed for saving.
- **Remembered UI values** (§4.8) live in `ui.toml` at the top of the sketch folder, written in a readable, stable order so diffs are meaningful. Neither Odin's `core:` nor its `vendor:` collection has a TOML package (checked 2026-10-24 against pkg.odin-lang.org), so rocinante writes its own small `toml` package in `roci/` that reads and writes the subset `ui.toml` and packaging settings use, and offers it upstream to Odin. It is committed to git, so a tuned performance travels with the repository. `forget ui` clears it. Packaged sketches start from the committed values.
- **System fonts are copied into the sketch** (decided 2026-10-24, revised 2026-11-02). When `font "Name"` finds no file in `assets/` but finds a font installed on the system, `roci run` copies the font file into `assets/fonts/` the first time and says so in one line, so the sketch looks the same on every machine and in every package. **The licence is the writer's business, not rocinante's** (decided 2026-11-02): the font's licence fields (the OpenType `name` table licence description and URL, and the `OS/2` embedding bits) are read only to warn. A font under a free licence (SIL Open Font License, Apache-2.0, the Ubuntu Font Licence, MIT, public domain) is copied with the one-line note; any other, or one that says nothing, is copied too, with the warning `Font_Licence` ("Check the licence of `Helvetica Neue` before you share your sketch"), and the sketch carries on. Nothing refuses to copy or package it (the earlier plan, to refuse a restrictive font in `roci package`, is dropped). **Built** (`roci/folder/systemfonts.odin`, `roci/interp/fonts.odin`): the file is copied under the name the sketch asked for, so the next run finds it in `assets/` on any computer; a lone file has no folder to copy into, so it only gets the warning; one file is copied, not the family's other styles (there is no `weight` yet). A name may also be one face of a collection (decided 2026-11-06): matched by the face's own full or PostScript name or its family and style, so `Helvetica Neue Condensed Black` reaches the face of `Helvetica Neue.ttc` that no file name says; the collection is the file that is copied.
- **Packaging details and versions: `sketch.toml`** (decided 2026-10-27). `roci package` creates it beside `sketch.roci` on first use, asking only for what it needs: app name, app id (bundle id), icon, version and build number. Writing it by hand is allowed and never required, and a sketch without it runs exactly the same, so non-negotiable 1 holds. It holds packaging details only, never anything that changes what the sketch does (window size and `fps` stay in the code). It uses the same `toml` package as `ui.toml`, and stays separate from it because `roci` writes `ui.toml` all the time while `sketch.toml` changes only when packaging.
  - **The version people see** is yours to choose: `1.0.1`, a date such as `2026.10.27`, or `version = "date"` to stamp the packaging day. `roci` checks it against each target's limits and explains in plain words when it does not fit (a date as one Windows number, for example).
  - **The build number** belongs to `roci`: it rises by one on every `roci package`, is stored in the file so it never goes down, and users never have to see it. The stores need it: Android's `versionCode` is a positive integer that must increase, at most 2,100,000,000 (Android docs, §12), Windows file and product versions are four numbers of at most 65,535 each (VERSIONINFO, §12), and Apple wants a short version plus a build number (to be read from Apple's documentation before the iOS and macOS packaging is written, because the page did not load when this was decided).
  - **Git tags are a check, not a requirement.** When the current commit has a `v…` tag that disagrees with the file, `roci package` says so and offers to fix one of them.
  - **Built for macOS** (2026-11-20, `roci/folder/settings.odin`, `cli/package.odin`): `name`, `id`, `icon`, `version` and `build`, and a `[macos]` table with `identity`, `notary-profile` and `category`; read and written with `roci/toml` (keys it does not know are kept, comments are not); `roci package` asks the name, id and version on a terminal when the file and the flags do not say (flag, then file, then default: the folder's name, `local.<slug>`, `1.0.0`), checks them (`check_name`, `check_id`, `check_version_apple`, `check_icon`, each in plain words), writes the file at once so it asks once, and raises `build` by one only after an app was made. Apple's rule, read 2026-11-20 from the archived Core Foundation Keys reference and the current glossary: the short version is up to three non-negative integers and the build is a whole number that rises (a suffix such as `1.0.0b12` is refused at upload, so none is accepted); `date` becomes `YYYY.M.D`. The git tag check is not done yet.

**`roci new` and `roci init`.** `roci new <name>` creates the folder above; `roci init` does the same in an existing folder without overwriting anything. Both:

- write `sketch.roci` (formatted, with the circle), `assets/` and its subfolders (each with an empty `.keep` file so git keeps them), `output/` and `.gitignore` (ignoring `output/` and roci's local cache);
- if git is installed, run `git init` and make a first commit; without git, say so in one line and carry on;
- **offer Git LFS** for large binary assets. On yes, and if `git lfs` is installed, track common audio, video, 3D, machine-learning and large image types (`*.wav`, `*.aiff`, `*.flac`, `*.mp4`, `*.mov`, `*.glb`, `*.fbx`, `*.tflite`, `*.litertlm`, `*.exr`, `*.psd`; finalised 2026-10-28 as `LFS_PATTERNS` in `roci/folder`: audio `wav aiff flac mp3 ogg`, video `mp4 mov webm mkv`, 3D `glb fbx`, models `tflite litertlm`, pictures `exr psd tif tiff`) with `git lfs track`, which writes `.gitattributes`, and commit that file. If Git LFS isn't installed, explain in plain words how to get it and continue without it. Git LFS is a separate, user-installed tool; rocinante neither bundles nor depends on it (§3).

### 4.10 Machine learning (decided 2026-10-24)

Trained models run locally, out of the box, in every sketch and on every platform. No cloud service, no account, no Python while the sketch runs. Users see pictures, numbers and lists, never tensors.

- **One runtime: LiteRT** (Google's on-device runtime, formerly TensorFlow Lite; Apache-2.0), used through its C API. The LiteRT repository (v2.3.0, 2026-10-07) has two: the classic one, `tflite/c/c_api.h`, which the original TensorFlow Lite C API became, and a newer `litert/c/` API built around `CompiledModel`, which Google's docs now lead with. Phase 6's spike picks one and records why; the classic one is the likelier fit for CPU inference with fixed threads (§9). Odin's `vendor:` collection has no machine-learning runtime, so the bindings live in `vendor/` (§3). One runtime keeps one behaviour (non-negotiable 7) and one set of bugs.
- **Three kinds of model, one file format.** LiteRT runs `.tflite` models, with `.litertlm` for language models. The other two sources reach that format through Google's official converters, once, on the author's machine:
  - **LiteRT:** `.tflite` and `.litertlm` files work as they are: drop them in `assets/ml-models/` or run `roci add pose.tflite`.
  - **PyTorch:** converted to `.tflite` with the LiteRT Torch converter.
  - **JAX:** converted with `jax2tf` to a TensorFlow SavedModel, then to `.tflite` with the TFLite converter. Google's docs call `jax2tf` experimental.
  - The converters are Python tools the author installs (non-negotiable 2's second exception). `roci doctor ml` checks for them and prints the install steps. Phase 0 settles which saved PyTorch and JAX files `roci add` can convert by itself and which need the short Python recipe in rocinante's docs.
- **Built-in tasks for the common cases**, in the spirit of ml5.js: `poses`, `hands`, `faces`, `objects`, `labels` (image classification) and `cutout` (person segmentation), each taking an image, a canvas or the webcam and giving plain data (lists of records with named points as vectors, labels as text, masks as images). They use open, permissively licensed models chosen in Phase 6. The first use of a task fetches its model from the URL recorded in rocinante's model list, checks its checksum, and copies it into the sketch's `assets/ml-models/` (so `roci run` needs the network once), like `use` vendors libraries (§5.10), so the sketch runs offline and travels whole.
- **Any other model:** `style = load "style.tflite"` loads it and `out = predict style webcam` runs it. `predict` reads the model's input and output description and converts automatically: images are resized and normalised, numbers and lists become tensors and back, image-shaped outputs come back as images. When a model doesn't describe its inputs well enough, the error teaches and shows how to say it with connecting words.
- **Never blocks a frame.** Inference runs on worker threads (§4.4). `predict` and the task verbs give the latest finished result, and an empty value until the first one arrives (§5.2 useful zero values), so a slow model lowers its own update rate, never the sketch's frame rate.
- **Hardware.** CPU (XNNPACK) everywhere; GPU through LiteRT's Metal (Apple) and WebGPU (desktop) paths; NPUs where LiteRT supports them. Never LiteRT's OpenGL path (non-negotiable 9); on Android that leaves OpenCL, the NPU and the CPU. Users never choose: the core picks the fastest path that works and falls back silently.
- **Equivalence and testing.** Interpreted and compiled sketches call the same runtime through the stable verb ABI. Equivalence and golden tests run models on the CPU with a fixed thread count, where results repeat exactly (§9).
- **Model licences.** Model weights carry their own licences. `roci add` records where a model came from and its licence in a small `.source` file next to it, and `roci package` lists the licences of every bundled model.
- **Platform gaps.** LiteRT's own list covers Android, iOS, Linux, macOS and Windows, but not FreeBSD or OpenBSD. FreeBSD's ports tree carries the Python TFLite runtime (`misc/py-tflite`), so building the C runtime there is likely; Phase 0 confirms it. If it fails, the fallback is ONNX Runtime (MIT, `misc/onnxruntime` in ports), decided then and recorded here. OpenBSD is settled by the same spike.
- **Inference only.** Training models inside rocinante is out of scope for the first stable release.

### 4.11 Video (decided 2026-10-24)

Playing and recording video uses no FFmpeg (its licensing conflicts with §3 unless it is an optional, user-installed component).

- **Each platform's own media framework first:** AVFoundation on macOS and iOS, Media Foundation on Windows, MediaCodec on Android. They decode H.264 and HEVC in hardware, and the operating system covers those formats' licensing.
- **Open formats everywhere through permissive libraries:** AV1 with dav1d (BSD-2-Clause), VP8 and VP9 with libvpx (BSD-3-Clause), MPEG-1 with pl_mpeg (MIT). On Linux, FreeBSD and OpenBSD, which have no system framework, these are the formats that play. AV1 and VP9 were designed to be royalty-free.
- **Recording** (`record`) writes AV1 or VP9 on every platform, and H.264 where the platform encoder offers it. Encoder libraries are chosen in Phase 6 under §3's rules.
- **FFmpeg as an optional extra:** if the user has installed FFmpeg, `movie` can use it for formats nothing else plays. rocinante never ships it, and `roci doctor` explains how to install it.
- `movie` and `webcam` give frames that work anywhere an image does: drawn, used as fills (§5.9), or passed to `predict` (§4.10).
- **Webcam built 2026-11-19** (the first part of Phase 6 item 3): `webcam` is a picture of what the first camera sees now, from SDL3's camera API (no new library); `image webcam`, `fill webcam`, `flip webcam`, a `snapshot = webcam` kept as it was, and the warning `Webcam_Unavailable` when there is no camera, it was refused, the one picked is not there or its pictures cannot be read; C has `roci_webcam`, `roci_webcam_camera_count`, `roci_webcam_camera_name`, `roci_webcam_pick_number` and `roci_webcam_pick`. `webcam cameras` lists the cameras and `webcam pick` chooses one (a number or part of a name; `pick` and not `to`, because `add webcam to shots` would then read as the verb). A camera's own size and rate, a mirror setting and recording the camera are not offered yet. `docs/design.md` code map, Webcam, has the details.
- **Movies built 2026-11-19** (the second part of Phase 6 item 3, the picture of MPEG-1 only): `clip = load "zoom.mpg"` (`.mpg` or `.mpeg`) is a picture that changes as it plays, so `image`, `fill`, `flip`, `resize` and the rest take it; `play`, `loop`, `pause`, `rewind [by]`, `forward by` and `set clip at 10 speed 2 loop true` move it (the sound verbs' words, a second shape of each verb with a picture first), and it reads `clip.playing`, `clip.at`, `clip.length`, `clip.speed` and `clip.loop`. Decoded by pl_mpeg (MIT, approved by Fabrizio 2026-11-19, copied from commit c871f2b into `vendor/sound/c`); the sound of a movie, the other formats (dav1d for AV1, libvpx for VP8 and VP9, the system frameworks for H.264) and recording to VP9 are the next steps. A movie's value is its own number, which never changes, and every verb that takes a picture asks for the picture of the moment, so `still = clip` is the movie and not a snapshot of it. The unreadable-file message is `Movie_Unreadable`; C has `roci_load_movie`, `roci_load_movie_file`, `roci_movie_frame` (the picture to draw) and the verbs. Checked by hand on a Mac only: Linux, FreeBSD, OpenBSD, Windows and the phones build the same C file and are untried.

### 4.12 The documentation site and the CI tools

Moved here from the code map in `CLAUDE.md`, which keeps a one-line pointer to each.

- `site/` and `tools/site` (the documentation, published at https://rocinante-lang.org by `.github/workflows/docs.yml`, which builds it on every push to `main` on GitHub and copies it with rsync to Fabrizio's server over a key that can write only the site's folder, the key, the address and the host key being the secrets `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY`, `DEPLOY_HOST` and `DEPLOY_KNOWN_HOSTS`; decided 2026-10-30, the GitHub Wiki being the other choice, turned down because it is unreviewed, not tied to a release and copied by hand from the registry; the structure follows Processing's reference and the guides of openFrameworks and Cinder): `site/hugo` is the Hugo project (our own theme in system fonts, except the front page's headings, set in Inter, the face rocinante draws text with, which `write_fonts` copies from `roci/gfx/fonts` so git keeps one copy; the front page, `layouts/home.html`, opens with three real windows, WILDVEX's title screen kept as `static/img/wildvex.webp` because the game is not in this repository and the voyage and studio examples, whose titles count their lines, which a test checks, then gives each thing that is built a band with an example's picture, and says only what is built, decided 2026-11-22), `site/guides/*.md` the hand-written guides, `site/pages/*.md` pages of their own at the top of the site (the FAQ, coloured like a guide, no pictures), `site/reference/<word>.roci` one example per reference word. `tools/site` copies the Hugo project to `output/site` and writes the rest: a page per word of `registry.REFERENCE_SECTIONS` from the registry (the same data as `roci man`), each example's picture drawn headless in a 400 by 300 window (`roci.set_default_window_size`), the messages page from `tests/errors`, and the guides with every `roci` block coloured by `lang.highlight`. The tab icon and the header's and footer's logo are made from `docs/logo/rocinante-logo.svg` (`write_logo`), never kept by hand. The README's title is the header's wordmark (the logo and the name in bold monospace) as a `<picture>` of two committed SVGs, `docs/logo/rocinante-wordmark.svg` and `-dark.svg` in GitHub's light and dark colours (a README cannot use generated files or styles): `odin run tools/site -- --logo` writes them from the logo, and a test fails when they are out of date. The word has a fixed `textLength`, so any reader's monospace font fits it. The same command draws `docs/logo/rocinante-logo-192.png` (on nothing) and `-192-white.png` (on white), for places that take no SVG, on the CPU with no graphics card (`logo_png.odin`: a small SVG reader that knows only what the logo uses, groups, paths of lines and arcs, circles, translate and scale, and says so when the logo uses anything else; geom strokes the outline; 16 by 16 samples a pixel by the nonzero rule), and a test compares them pixel by pixel, a channel allowed one step off. An example gets a picture when it draws shapes or its widgets drew something (`roci.ui_drawn`; a hidden interface draws nothing), so a page whose example is only a `ui` block shows its widgets. Examples that move are recorded (`animate.odin`): ten seconds at 30 frames a second, a frame drawn only when `roci.frame_hash` changes (which counts what the interface draws, `ui.look_hash`: its triangles, so a progress bar filling up or a hovered button is a change), as an APNG that plays unless the reader asks for reduced motion; an example that never changes is found without being drawn. An example that reads the mouse, the wheel or fingers (`uses_input`) gets a visitor: the mouse on a slow figure of eight, four clicks that are also a finger, the wheel turned up and down, drawn into the recording as a pointer and a ring. A guide's picture is described by the section it is in (`last_heading`), and every guide and page needs a `summary:` of at most 155 characters, which a test checks. What search engines and shared links read is `_partials/seo.html`: the title, the page's summary as its description, its canonical address, Open Graph cards (a reference word shows its own example's picture, other pages the logo drawn by rocinante), structured data (the software on the home page, breadcrumbs elsewhere), and Hugo's sitemap with a robots.txt naming it; all of it follows `baseURL`. Links to a guide's or the FAQ's question go by the anchor Hugo makes from its heading (the home page links `faq/#why-another-language-when-ai-can-code-anything`), so rewording a heading breaks them: search `site/` for the old anchor. Generated pages are Markdown whose `<pre>` blocks are one line (`one_line`), because a blank line ends an HTML block and Hugo refuses `.html` content by default. Tests fail when a word has no example, an example does not check or is not formatted, or a guide's code block has an error or warning (`check_files_test.odin`); `roci/registry` fails when a word is in no section or two. **The examples** (`tools/site/showcase.odin`) are a section of their own: the sketches of `examples/`, grouped by what they show (`SHOWCASE_CATEGORIES`, and `SHOWCASE` lists every sketch with its title, category, frames and windows; `SHOWCASE_LEFT_OUT` those that are not there and why, and a test fails when a folder is in neither). A page is made from the sketch itself, run in its own folder with the golden images' frame counts: the comment at the top of the sketch is its explanation (so it must be written for a reader, never pointing at something outside the sketch), its first sentences are the summary the index and the search show, every window it opens is drawn at its own size, a picture that moves is recorded moving (5 seconds at 15 frames a second, halved when the window is wider than 480 pixels, tried again smaller and then dropped when it would pass 2 MB, which is what a shader of noise does), the code is shown with its lines numbered in a pane for each file (the comment left out, libraries last), and the words it uses are links to the reference. **The search** is the script `site/hugo/assets/js/site.js` over `index.json`, which `layouts/index.json` makes of every page (the reference and the examples by title and summary, the guides, the FAQ and the tool pages with the start of their text and a line for each heading, the messages by title through the `entries` of their front matter): a panel under the bar opened by the button, `/` or Ctrl or Cmd with K, the file fetched when it is first opened, every word asked for found in a page's title, summary or text, results as links with the words marked, arrow keys and Enter. **The menu**: below 900 pixels, and only where the script runs, the five words fold behind a button (a panel under the bar, closed by a link, Escape or a click outside); without the script they stay in the bar. Hugo is a build tool only, pinned in `HUGO_VERSION` and installed by `tools/ci/install-hugo.sh` (checksum checked). `docs serve` runs `tools/site --watch` (`watch.odin`): it keeps the reference pages already in `output/site`, starts `hugo server` and copies or rewrites only the files of `site/` that change; a change to the registry is Odin code and needs a new start.
- `tools/ci/`: `install-odin.sh` installs the release in `ODIN_VERSION` for both pipelines; `install-sdl3.sh` builds the latest stable SDL3 for GitHub (GitHub gets lavapipe from Ubuntu's `mesa-vulkan-drivers`). `install-spirv-cross.sh` builds the pinned SPIRV-Cross C library for GitHub, whose Ubuntu package lacks the functions the Metal translation calls (the GitHub jobs cache the build under a key that holds the script's hash). `orion.Dockerfile` is the image ORION's jobs run in (`rocinante-ci`, built on ORION with the command at its top, and again now and then to bring Arch up to date): Arch with `clang`, `sdl3`, `diffutils`, `vulkan-swrast` and the rest already installed and the pinned Odin in `/opt/odin`, because installing about a gigabyte with pacman in every job took minutes on ORION's two-core Athlon (decided 2026-11-01). A job uses the image's Odin when it is the one in `ODIN_VERSION` and fetches it otherwise, so a new pin never waits for the image; a new package for the pipeline goes in the Dockerfile and needs a rebuild.

### 4.13 The code map

Moved here from `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-11-16, which names the packages and points here. Only what the code does not say itself: invariants, traps and why. Keep each entry to a few lines when you add one; the code and its tests hold the rest.

- `build/`: `ci` is a list of `step` calls in `check_packages`, so a new package or spike joins the pipelines there and in `TEST_PACKAGES`; `check_optimised_tests` repeats every test with `-o:speed -use-separate-modules` (a unit for each package: Odin builds an optimised program as one unit by default, which LLVM works through on one thread, 29 s for `roci/interp` against 3 s so, the repeat having taken 43 minutes of the runner's hour; the compiled sketches of the golden images are still built as one unit) except `roci/builds`, which builds and runs the same programs whatever flags the test has (`NOT_REPEATED_OPTIMISED`); the sketches those tests and the golden images compile are built as ONE program (`codegen.bundle_main`, `Options.package_name`, `bundle_package`: each sketch its own package in its own folder, a root `main` that runs the one named by the first argument), so the core is compiled once and not once for each sketch; `check_tracked_fonts` fails when git tracks a font rocinante does not ship. The libraries `ci` makes first (Dear ImGui, the sound decoders, libcurl; accesskit-c has its own marker) are kept while the stamp written beside each (`build/stamp.odin`, `sources_stamp`: a hash of the names and contents of the scripts and C sources it is made from, plus `ODIN_VERSION` for the decoders) is the stamp of those sources now, not by modification times, which a fresh checkout breaks; a library with no stamp is built once more, which writes one. That is what lets both pipelines cache only the libraries and their stamps (`.gitlab-ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, which no longer build them as steps of their own): Dear ImGui took 51 s, the decoders 51 s and libcurl about as long again to build on ORION in every job. `build/golden.odin` runs `tests/golden` on lavapipe only (the loader gets just the `lvp_icd*.json` manifests, and it fails unless the device says llvmpipe).
- `spikes/` is throwaway Phase 0 code (`spikes/codegen` is the pipeline in miniature, `spikes/ui` is not in `ci`).
- `roci` (the public Odin API): the verbs over one thread-local sketch, `the`. Verbs act on the *current window*: the default window while setting up (made by the first verb), the window whose `draw` runs during a frame, otherwise the window the last `window` call made. `step(events, dt)` is one frame and drives a **headless** sketch (`start_headless`, no SDL; `dt` left out is a sixtieth, or one frame at the sketch's `fps`); it runs the frame in memory of its own (`context.temp_allocator` inside the frame) and frees it when the frame ends, headless or not, writing `0xDD` over it first in tests (`poison_frames`), so a pointer from long-lived data into frame memory fails in a test; what the caller made with its own temporary allocator is left alone. `api_table.odin` says which Odin procedure answers each registry verb (a test fails when one is missing). Ctrl/Cmd+Q quit; Escape does not.
  - Mouse buttons and fingers are held **per window** (`Win.buttons`, `Win.fingers`); fingers are numbered from `next_finger` and never reused in a run; losing focus releases every key and button. `events.odin` keeps the frame's log; `set_frame_hook` runs the interpreter's `on`, `every` and messages before the window's `draw`. `overlay.odin` draws the error band after the frame is built and takes it off the list again (`gfx.mark`, `gfx.rewind`). `begin_restart`/`end_restart` hand the open windows to a restarted sketch in order.
  - `window_settings.odin` holds every window setting (remembered on the `Win` so headless can answer, handed to SDL when there is a window, put back by a restart); the language's view is `registry.WINDOW_PROPERTIES`.
  - **Canvases** (`canvases.odin`): while `draw into` is on, `current_list`, `width` and `height` are the canvas's; `flush_canvases` runs at the start of every `show_frame` (with `nil` when headless, where rounds wait until `picture` flushes them on its own device, so `picture` of a window with canvases needs a GPU). `canvas` makes no window: it reads the density of the window that exists (`existing_win`), else takes 2, so a canvas made in a constant, before the sketch's `window` line, does not open a default window that the line would then resize (found 2026-11-26, #75; a Mac stopped the program when that was a fullscreen resize, which `fit_to_screen` also holds off now).
  - **Pictures and pixels**: `draw.odin` holds `fill_image` (`Paint` is a colour, gradient or `Image_Fill`; `stroke` and `background` take a picture, text fills compose on the CPU); `WORD_CHOICES` keeps the picture shape's word list under `"fill picture"`. `gfx/picture_ops.odin` is pure CPU work on RGBA bytes with alpha multiplied in first; `pixels.odin` remembers made pictures in `the.derived` by a hash of what made them and `evict_derived` lets go of the longest unused when the made pictures pass `DERIVED_BUDGET_BYTES` (each counted twice, pixels and texture) or `MAX_DERIVED_PICTURES`, down to 90% of it, and costs two comparisons in a frame within the limits (a let-go picture is nil in `image_data`). A picture's number is its store slot plus a generation (`Image_Id`, under 2^24 because commands carry it as a float): a slot let go is reused only after 256 others wait, and a stale number names nothing; `released_images` is cut once every renderer has read it (`image_reader_add`); a canvas is read back by `gfx.canvas_read` (a fence wait, and the read is kept in `the.canvas_reads` until the canvas is drawn again, so it costs once per change however many verbs read it). `mask_picture` with a canvas goes to `mask_on_gpu` (`gfx/renderer_mask.odin`): its key is the two numbers and the canvases' `drawn`, it must draw the canvases' waiting rounds before its pass (`flush_canvases`) so the mask shows them as drawn up to its line, it submits through `canvas_submit` and never waits, and what it makes is a fixed canvas whose bytes are its one texture (`mask_bytes`); an evicted one also drops its entry of `the.canvas_reads` (`forget_derived`). `note_mask_made` keeps the newest mask of each picture and stencil (`the.mask_last`) and puts the one it replaces in `the.masks_replaced`, which `let_go_replaced_masks` (first thing in `evict_derived`) lets go once it is two frames undrawn and unread, since otherwise a mask made every frame stayed until 256 MB of them had piled up; and when a renderer lets a fixed canvas go, its texture goes to `mask_pool` (at most `MASK_POOL_MOST`, the oldest released past that) and `mask_on_gpu` draws into one of the same size before it makes a texture, which took about 0.8 ms on Direct3D 12 (the pass clears it, and the GPU runs a renderer's work in order, so an earlier frame's use of it is finished first). `Shader_Data.textures` is 2 for the mask, so its pipeline asks for two samplers, and the Metal translation numbers textures and samplers 0 and 1. `tests/bench/mask-canvas` is its benchmark, its MODE line splitting the read-back, the masks and the drawing. `set_graphics_refused` is the test knob for that message.
  - **Effects and shaders** (`effects.odin`): `add_shader` translates at once so a mistake is known (the back ends add every shader the program names before its first frame: `lang/check_shaders.odin`, `interp/effects.odin` `warm_shaders`, `rt/effects.odin` `warm_shaders`); `effect` runs a shader over the finished frame or over the canvas being drawn into; `interp/effects.odin` matches a record's fields to the shader's globals by name and retranslates when the file's modification time changes. `outline.odin` makes the solid mask of a picture for `outline` (a canvas gives nothing).
  - **`save` and `record`** (`save.odin`, `record.odin`): `save` queues paths on the window and `show_frame` writes them after the frame is built and before the overlay (so a saved picture never shows an error band); `.svg` and `.pdf` come from the command list with no GPU, and what they leave out is a `Save_Problem` with `approximate` (said as `Save_Approximate`). `record` hands every finished frame to `record_frame`; an `.apng` is kept in memory (512 MB) and written at the end, a video is piped to the user's `ffmpeg` (`find_ffmpeg`, test knob `set_ffmpeg`). `write_all` retries when Odin's `write` gives up on a signal, and `ignore_broken_pipes` stops SIGPIPE ending the sketch. Tests get frames from `set_frame_source` (a real draw would start SDL on a test thread); tests that start the stand-in ffmpeg are the package `roci/movies`, because forking among roci's many test threads killed the child about a run in three on macOS.
  - `touch.odin` sets `SDL_HINT_MOUSE_TOUCH_EVENTS` so the left button is a finger on the desktop; `turtle.odin` keeps the turtle on the list beside the transform (home is the middle of the current window, worked out at each move).
- `roci/abi*.odin`, `include/roci.h`, `examples/c/*.c`: the C ABI (the programs that show it, built by `tools/abi-check.sh` with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` and taught by `site/guides/40-rocinante-from-c.md`: `circle`, `scene`, `controls`, `player`, `kaleidoscope` (the file dialog, a picture as a triangle's fill, mirrored turns), `reaction` with `gray_scott.c`, a reaction-diffusion library written for it that knows nothing of rocinante, and `packing` with `stb_rect_pack.h` v1.02 (the three ran on a Mac, checked by Fabrizio 2026-11-25; Linux and the BSDs are untried), copied from commit 48fb1c9 of github.com/nothings/stb, MIT or public domain, for examples only and so not in `tools/licences.txt`, which lists what ships; added 2026-11-25); **full coverage is a rule**: every verb and feature gets its C functions in the same branch, tests included; `abi.odin` has the first drawing verbs, `abi_window.odin` windows, input and events, `abi_pictures.odin` gradients, pictures, pixels, save, record, shaders and fonts, `abi_collide.odin` collision and trees of boxes, `abi_ui.odin` the widgets, `abi_misc.odin` messages, tweens, random helpers, style scopes and the headless run, and each file has its `abi_*_group` that lists its functions in the table; `ABI_LANGUAGE` in `abi_header.odin` lists every language-only verb with its C functions or the reason C needs none, and a test fails for a verb in neither, so a new verb cannot skip C), one plain `roci_<verb>` function per shape (colours four floats 0 to 255, `roci_window_id` because `roci_window` is the function). `ABI_TABLE` lists every function; `ABI_NOT_IN_C` lists shapes with no C form and why. The 3D verbs are in C since 2026-11-14 (`roci_vec3` for points in space; the registry's `Vector` kind is two to four numbers, so `ABI_WIDE` says which `_v` parameters and results are three wide; lights, `material`, `model` and `mesh` have hand-written prototypes because their registry shapes take words or a file name; `roci_load_model` and `roci_load_svg` read the file from disk by path and keep it by path and time as the interpreter does, `roci/svg.odin` holding the drawings; `roci_ray` gives the line through a pixel, `roci_triangles` copies a model's triangles into the caller's array and says how many there are, so a C program can do its own picking, since the collision verbs are not in C). Tests fail when a registry shape has neither, a parameter type differs, or the header is stale (`odin run tools/header -- include/roci.h`). `tools/abi-check.sh` exit 3 means it cannot run here (`ci` fails instead when `ROCI_REQUIRE_SDL3` is set).
- `roci/maths`: colours, the one random generator (SplittableRandom), noise, and `sin_deg`/`cos_deg` (exact at right angles, never -0, the same on every system); both back ends call all of it. `ease.odin`: `EASE_NAMES` is kept equal to the registry's words by a test.
- `roci/registry`: the data the language is made from: `VERBS` (Odin API and C header) and `LANGUAGE_VERBS` (interpreter only) make `ALL_VERBS`, built once at start because lookups return pointers into it. `labels.odin` holds the label forms (`circle at 100 100 radius 50` is the shape `x y radius`); `WINDOW_PROPERTIES`; `manual.odin`; `VERB_SYNONYMS` (Logo's `fd` suggests `forward`); `words.odin` `WORD_CHOICES` (a test fails when a word argument has no list). A new verb is a row in `verbs.odin`, its procedure in `roci`, an `api_table.odin` entry and a C entry or `ABI_NOT_IN_C` reason.
- `roci/lang` (grammar in `docs/grammar.ebnf`; no GPU): lexer, `prescan` (reads `learn` headers first), error-tolerant parser (one pass reports every error), checker, typed IR, error catalogue, `report.odin`, `json.odin`, `format.odin`, `highlight.odin`. The files of a sketch folder are **joined into one `Source`** (`join_sources`; only `place_of` knows file and line); `order_files`: library files, other files by name, `sketch.roci` last; only `sketch.roci` may hold statements that run. Every message id needs a sketch in `tests/errors/<id>/` (runtime ones in `tests/errors/runtime/`, run by `roci/interp`); `ROCI_UPDATE_ERRORS=1 odin test roci/lang` rewrites them, review like code. A message's words never hold a bare bracket; `GRAMMAR_RULES` in `parser.odin` is compared with the grammar file by a test.
  - **Parsing traps**: `Label_Form.lead` values are written by position before the label words (`text "hello" at 20 20`). A call ends at its largest shape's argument count, at the end of the line, at a closing bracket, and in a one-line block at two spaces. A connecting word starts an option only once the required arguments are filled (so `width` is a value in `rect 0 0 width height`); a connecting word the call does not take, where a value is still needed, is `Reserved_Name`. Where a verb takes a word (`caps round`), the parser takes it as written even if it is also a verb. After a per-element `fill`/`stroke` value, `parse_paint_words` takes `as`, `size`, `at`, `in`, `angle`, `stops` as that paint's by position alone (`as corner`, and `as center` after a plain color, stay the shape's origin).
  - **Teaching errors the parser builds from the writer's line**: `Needs_Brackets`, `Word_Outside_Brackets`, `Index_Brackets`, `Unknown_Option`, `Hash_Comment`, `Colours_Expected`, `Title_Of_Window`, `Place_After_Name`, `Labels_Needed`, `Comma_In_Call`; the lexer says `Habit_Operator` for `==`, `!=`, `&&`, `||` and `!` and emits the `Habit_*` token that stands for the word (`is`, `is not`, `and`, `or`, `not`), so nothing cascades; the checker builds `Set_Window`.
  - **The checker** runs twice (the first pass settles record shapes, merged by `unify`, and the types of the sketch-wide names, which the second pass starts from, so a verb checked above the line that says what an empty list holds still knows it: `settled`, found 2026-11-17 when a shot of swarm read as having no position); a word a verb takes (`around z`) is the word even where the sketch has a variable of that name (`as_word`), so it means the same wherever the variable is written; every name assigned anywhere is sketch-wide from the start, capitals mean constant, an `Error` type fits everything so one mistake gives one message. The loop name of `for each` and `for` is the loop's own: `collect_assignments` does not make a sketch-wide name of it where the body assigns it (that is a write-back to the item), and `Symbol.mutated` on it makes `for each` over a constant list `Constant_Changed_By_Verb`. A `learn` is checked once per distinct tuple of argument types (`IR_Instance`, cached before its body is checked, which is how recursion ends; a verb that calls itself with a bigger value each time, `learn f x [ f [ x ] ]`, would never end that way, so past `MAX_OPEN_INSTANCES` versions inside one another the arguments are taken as mixed values). `check`/`expect` are `IR_Check`/`IR_Expect` (a check is a definition any file may hold; its names are its own). Collision shapes get a `Shape_Class` from the literal's field names (`check_collide.odin`).
  - **The formatter** formats the tree and keeps literals as written; where the style leaves a choice the sketch's choice stands, so formatting twice changes nothing. A test formats every example and `tests/check` sketch and compares trees.
- `roci/interp`: a tree walker over the typed IR calling the same `roci` procedures an Odin sketch calls; `load` runs `lang.optimise` (passes shared with the future code generator; `ROCI_PASSES=off` is the reference, and a test compares every example both ways). A number-typed value can hold the empty value, so only a `Sure_Number` is stored or passed as a plain number. **Memory**: sketch set-up values live in the `values` arena, block values in the temporary allocator freed each frame; `promote` deep-copies into the arena when stored in a sketch-wide name or long list; `collect.odin` copies live values into the other arena between frames. Every walk over a value (`promote`, `deep_copy`, the collector, reload's `convert`, `equal`) uses a list of work and no recursion, so a chain of 100,000 records is safe, and the copies copy each list, record and table once, so sharing and self-holding values survive; a new walk must do the same. **Runtime failures never stop a sketch** (empty value or 0 plus a `Runtime_Error`, said once per place; recursion 100 deep and a block over its 2-second budget too). On Windows, which gives a program and each thread one megabyte of stack, a taught call is also refused with `Recursion_Too_Deep` when less than `STACK_ROOM` (128 KB) is left above the end `GetCurrentThreadStackLimits` gives (`stack_windows.odin`, read at the outermost call): a call costs 4 to 8 KB and up to 20 KB with blocks inside blocks in it (measured 2026-11-27), so a hundred calls through nested `push` blocks overflowed the stack there and ended the program; elsewhere the stack is eight megabytes and the count is the only limit. `push` and `draw into` are run by `call_push` and `draw_into` from `eval_call`, outside `call_builtin`, whose frame holds the room of every verb. Verbs are a switch on `Verb_Code` (`codes.odin`; switching on names cost a fifth of a busy frame), and a field is read, made and set by its place in the shape when the `Field_Slots` pass found it (`IR_Field.at`, guarded by the record's shape, by name otherwise); a test fails when the registry gains a verb with no code. `send` copies the value into one of two message arenas. `give` and `stop` are a `Flow` status, never an exception.
  - **Reload** (`reload.odin`): a reload makes a **new `Interp`**, retires the old one next frame, and carries values (top-level statements matched by their text; `convert`/`compatible`); a changed statement that opens a window or draws while setting up (`is_effect`) restarts the sketch instead. `watch` of a `sketch.roci` watches every file of its folder.
  - **Soak and fuzz** (`soak_test.odin`): a sketch that makes a picture every frame runs 10,000 frames with the pictures held bounded, and every example, changed by typing mistakes from a generator with a fixed seed, is read, checked and run three frames; a message is the answer, a crash or a leak fails the run (the bounds of `maths.rng_int` are both included, and the examples are taken in sorted order, because a folder's own order differs between systems and so did the changes: the first Linux run found what the Mac's had not). A long run for a new kind of change is worth doing by hand once (400 changes for each example is about a minute).
  - **Checks** (`checks.odin`): `run_checks` is what `roci test` calls; `p.events` counts every `fail` call even when left out as already said; `Runtime_Error.in_check` keeps what a check ran into off the overlay.
  - **Libraries**: `use "name"` joins `libs/name/`; verbs are found by `library/name` (`learn_key`); a call in a library finds that library's own verb first, then the sketch's, then a library's.
  - **Collision** (`collide.odin`): `tree_for` keeps a box tree for a list asked about twice unchanged (`Interp.writes`/`next_write` stamps); answers must equal checking every shape in list order. Empty `contact` and `cast` results are records of zeros.
  - **Fonts** (`fonts.odin`): `font "Mine"` finds Inter's faces, then `assets/` (`folder.find_asset`), then an installed font (`folder.find_system_font`, test knob `folder.system_font_dirs`), or one face of a collection by its full, PostScript or family-and-style name (`folder.face_named`; the face number goes to SDL_ttf as a property and to the PDF reader). The font is copied into `assets/fonts/` with a notice; one not known to be free gets `Font_Licence` (sharing is the writer's problem). **Tests must never call `fonts_stop`** (it ends SDL_ttf for every thread), and a thread keeps the fonts it added, so use unique font names or PostScript names. `roci/gfx/emoji.odin` draws emoji apart from the text.
  - **Pictures** (`images.odin`): `Interp.pictures` remembers each name so a draw loop costs one `stat` a frame; the thread-local `decoded_files` lets a reload find what the old interpreter decoded. Failures are `Image_Missing` and `Image_Unreadable`. **Tweens** keep their path to the place on the heap and run in the frame hook before any handler; a new interpreter starts with none. **Tables** keep names in insertion order.
- `roci/folder`: sketch folders (`read_program` reads every `.roci` file in joining order, not `assets`, `libs`, `output` or hidden folders), `new_sketch`/`init_sketch` (never overwrite), `add_asset`, `find_asset` (two files of a name are `Ambiguous`), libraries (`get_library`, `update_library`; git is the user's tool and no test uses the network), system fonts. `make_directory_all` reports `.Exist` for a folder that is there, so use `ensure_directory`.
- `cli` (the `roci` binary): `run`/`check` take a file or a folder; `run --frames N` is headless and can `--screenshot`; `run --errors json` in a window writes each `print` as a line `{"event":"print","text":...}` on stdout and keeps none of them (`Interp.print_json`; `printed` is only for tests and headless runs, because a sketch printing every frame would otherwise fill memory); `test` unit tests must not draw (starting the graphics device starts SDL's video system, which among the other tests' threads hung a pre-push run and crashed another, 2026-11-11), so they refuse graphics (`roci.set_graphics_refused`), and the full `ci` runs `roci test examples/checks` as a program of its own on lavapipe (`check_stored_pictures` in `build/golden.odin`), which has to draw and match its stored picture; storing a changed picture is checked by hand (`roci test --accept`, change the sketch, `roci test`). `roci man` shows the hand-written sketch of `site/reference` for a word (built in by `registry.reference_example`, which `#load_directory`s the folder), and a call made of stand-in values only when the site has none; it also has pages for the built-in values and the events.
- `site/` and `tools/site`: the documentation, built by Hugo from the registry, the examples and `site/guides/*.md` (design.md §4.12). Tests fail when a word has no example, an example does not check or a guide has no `summary:`. Every sketch of `examples/` is on the site (`SHOWCASE` in `tools/site/showcase.odin`) or left out with a reason, and the comment at its top is the text of its page, so write it for a reader; the search and the menu are `site/hugo/assets/js/site.js` (design.md §4.12).
- `tools/term`: `out`, `err`, `message`, `out_icon`; colour only on a terminal with `NO_COLOR` unset. `setup` is called first in every `main`. `progress.odin` draws a long process as steps (decided 2026-11-22, the live style with an emoji per kind of step chosen by Fabrizio from previews): `progress_begin`, `progress_step` (ends the step before as done), `progress_fail`, `progress_finish` ("Ready in …") and `progress_result`; live (`out_colour`) the running step has its emoji, a spinner and its time redrawn about twelve times a second by a thread and becomes ✅ or ❌ with its time, otherwise each step is a plain line when it starts and when it ends. Nothing may print to standard output while a step runs, so whatever the step runs is captured. `roci run --compile` and `roci package` use it, and the phone scripts say `step <kind> <words>`, `result`, `finished` and `problem` lines that `put_on_phone` reads through a pipe, every other line going to the sketch's `artifacts/<target>.log`, so no tool's name or warning reaches the screen unless something fails.
- `roci/platform`: SDL3 and the GPU device. **`set_size` holds a Mac window to its screen** (`fit_to_screen`; found 2026-11-26, #75): AppKit stops the program with a breakpoint trap when `SDL_SetWindowSize` makes a window that already exists (a canvas made before the `window` line opens one) taller than its screen's usable area with the title bar, which a sketch that asks for a big size and then fills the screen does (`window size 3200 2000 fullscreen true` on a 1440-high screen); SDL says a window not shown yet has no borders, so 40 points are kept for the title bar then. On Windows the device is asked for as `direct3d12` by name with only DXIL and DXBC offered, so SDL never falls back to Vulkan; elsewhere SPIR-V and MSL are offered and SDL picks. On Wayland it asks SDL for `wayland,x11` unless `SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER` is set. SDL is shared by every `App`, so only the last `shutdown` calls `SDL_Quit` (others `SDL_QuitSubSystem`), and **a test build never calls `SDL_Quit`** (`when ODIN_TEST`; it hung one test run in twelve). On Android SDL counts window sizes in pixels (`WINDOW_IS_IN_PIXELS`). The device has clip distances off and SDL's debug mode off unless `ROCI_GPU_DEBUG` is set. The SDL calls are checked by hand with `examples/window`. `dialogs.odin` shows SDL's file dialogs without waiting (the answers sit in a locked table, taken by request number, because SDL calls back from any thread) and holds the clipboard and `open_address`; `roci/interp/dialogs.odin` runs the blocks (§5.19).
- Sound (`roci/audio/mixer.odin`, `roci/sound.odin`, `roci/platform/audio.odin`, `vendor/sound`, `roci/interp/sounds.odin`; §4.6): a sound is one player in the sketch's thread-local mixer (`Sound` is its place plus one), decoded whole by `vendor/sound` (a C wrapper over miniaudio's `ma_decode_memory`, built from the Odin install's own sources, so it needs no library in Odin's folder) into two floats a frame at 48,000 a second, and copied into the sketch's allocator so a test sees a leak. **A file longer than 30 seconds is streamed** (`roci.add_sound_file` by path, `add_sound` by bytes, which the decoder copies; `STREAM_MIN_SECONDS`, test knob `set_stream_threshold`): `audio.Stream` (`roci/audio/stream.odin`) is a ring of 8 seconds of frames in the sound's own timeline, read ahead by one thread per mixer (`stream_service`, 4,096 frames at a time, outside every lock but the stream's own, which the audio thread takes for one chunk only), with `vendor/sound`'s `roci_stream_*` over `ma_decoder_init_file` (the audio package sees only `audio.Source`, three procedures, so its tests use sounds made in code); frames that are missing are silence and `at` does not move, a seek keeps the ring when it holds the place, and a loop's seam is continuous because the thread wraps the decoder and `stream_wrapped` renumbers the ring; a mixer made `synchronous` (headless, `graphics_refused`) has no thread and reads inside `mix`. The thread is stopped and joined in `destroy_mixer` before a ring is freed, `stream_unregister` waits for it before a replaced stream is closed (never with the mixer's lock held), and a decoder's seek is only close to the frame asked for when the file is not at 48,000. The mixer is plain Odin behind one lock; SDL's audio thread calls `audio.mix` through `platform.open_audio`'s callback, which must allocate nothing. **The speakers open only in a sketch that has a window** (`open_speakers` skips headless runs and `set_graphics_refused`, so no test opens a device); a headless `step` mixes into scratch memory and throws it away (`sounds_step`), so `rain.at` in a test is what it would be in a window. `shutdown` closes the speakers before letting go of the mixer and raises `sound_epoch`. The interpreter keeps a decoded file by path across reloads (`decoded_sounds`), so a save does not restart the music, and a file saved again is decoded into the same sound (`replace_sound`). `load` gives a sound when its argument is a text literal ending in `.wav`, `.flac`, `.mp3` or `.ogg` (`is_sound_file` in the checker; a computed name is a picture). `volume`, `pan` and `speed` are free words (`SOUND_FREE_WORDS`), and a free word straight after a connecting word that has no value yet is that value (`play rain speed speed`). A verb whose shapes take different words (`set` of a window or a sound) says `Word_For_Other_Kind` for a word of the other shape, where it used to drop it silently. **Analysis** (`roci/audio/analysis.odin`): every player, the mix and the microphone keep their last 2048 frames in a `Ring` filled by `mix` (a stopped sound is cleared at once), and `level` and `bands` work on a copy outside the lock; a headless step mixes even when nothing plays, so a level falls to 0 as it does in a window. The microphone is `audio.MIC`, a sound number no player has (playing it does nothing), opened by the first `mic` in a sketch with a window (`platform.open_microphone`); tests give it frames with `feed_microphone`. **Instruments** (`roci/audio/synth.odin`): a player with a `Wave` instead of samples and up to 32 `Voice`s, each a note with its own fade (5 ms in, 80 ms out); `play` on one starts a voice (held, or `for` seconds), `pause` lets voices go and the player stops playing once they have faded, and a note on a sound from a file sets its speed as if it were middle C. `for` is a keyword and also a connecting word of `play` and `loop`: the call loop reads any word the call lists, so no parser change was needed. **MIDI** (`roci/platform/midi*.odin`, `roci/midi.odin`): our own backends, no library: the raw device files on Linux (`/dev/snd/midiC*D*`), FreeBSD (`/dev/umidi*`) and OpenBSD (`/dev/rmidi*`) read by one thread without blocking and looked for again every second, CoreMIDI on macOS and WinMM on Windows (both written from their documentation; every desktop backend, in and out, was checked by hand on its system by 2026-11-16), nothing on Android yet; `midi_parse` keeps running status and passes over real-time bytes and system exclusive. Reading starts with the first `on midi` handler, `midi control` or C `roci_on` of a MIDI event, never in a headless sketch or a test (`feed_midi` gives bytes instead). Events are logged against the focused window (or the first) so each handler runs once; velocities and values are 0 to 100. **The wait for the screen** (`wait_for_screens` in `roci/loop.odin`): before each `step`, every shown window's swapchain texture is asked for without blocking (`gfx.acquire_window`), and while it is not there yet `midi_early` runs the MIDI blocks of what was read (through `set_early_hook`, the interpreter's `early_hook` and C's `abi_early_hook`, which run only event handlers), beginning the target window's next frame first (`Win.begun`) so what they draw is in it; after 50 ms of "not yet" it falls back to the blocking acquire. `show_frame` draws into `Win.acquired`. A window whose acquired texture is not drawn must still be submitted (`destroy_win` does). **On NVIDIA the wait is in the submit** (measured: recording 0.05 ms, submit 9.8 ms at 105 Hz), which SDL requires on the main thread, so the non-blocking acquire never says "not yet" there; `start_late` and `paced` cover it: while MIDI is read and the first window has vsync, the loop sleeps after each frame until the next refresh less the frame's build time and a margin (`Pacing`; the refresh is learned from the frames, a missed refresh widens the margin by a millisecond, which shrinks back slowly), running `midi_early` every 0.25 ms. `pace_learn` is the arithmetic, tested without a clock. **SoundFonts** (`roci/audio/soundfont.odin`, our own reader): `font_read` flattens each preset into `Region`s (keys, velocities, a sample with its loop, root and tuning, gain, pan, envelope), an instrument with `Wave.Font` owns its `Font`, and each note starts a voice for every region that covers its key and the instrument's volume as velocity; a loop's end is the first frame after it. A damaged file must be refused or played without reading past the samples (a test changes bytes of `tests/sounds/tiny.sf2` 300 ways). `tools/soundfont` writes that font (`tiny`, compared byte for byte by its test), cuts FreePats' CC0 piano down for `examples/soundfont` (`cut`) and lists a font (`dump`). **Effects** (`roci/audio/effects.odin`): sixteen in one `effect_run` switch, settings by place in `EFFECTS` (names, start values); each sound is mixed alone into `Mixer.scratch` (`mix_chunk`, 1,024 frames at a time), through its effects, then added, and `speaker_effects` run over the sum; a stopped sound keeps running through its effects for `effect_tail` seconds (`Player.tail`), so echoes fade. An effect's memory (delay lines, reverb combs) is made outside the lock by `effect_put`; a delay's first time is taken at once and later changes glide. **Envelope and voices**: a player's `attack`, `decay`, `sustain` and `release` (-1 means the default) are a floor over a SoundFont region's own envelope (`envelope_level` takes the player); `free_voice` takes a free slot, then a released voice, then the quietest, then the oldest, skips the slots the same note already claimed, and a voice that is taken keeps its last samples as a short ghost (`STEAL_FADE`) so stealing never clicks. **Buffer**: `load "song.mp3" buffer 20` sets a stream's ring (`Stream.ring_frames`, 1 to 60 seconds, 8 when not written). **Limiter**: the device path sets `Mixer.limit` and `mix` bends what is above 0.8 (`soft_limit`) after the effects of `speakers`, so what is recorded is what is heard; without it `mix` is the exact sum. **Recording** (`roci/record_sound.odin`, `roci/audio/capture.odin`, `wav.odin`): `record speakers|sound|mic "x.wav"` starts a `Capture` (a 4-second ring of stereo floats, written by the audio thread inside `mix` and drained by the stream worker's thread, or inside `mix` itself when the mixer is synchronous so tests are exact; a full ring drops the newest and counts an overrun, said when the take ends); the sink is our own 16-bit WAV writer, or the user's ffmpeg (`-f f32le -ar 48000 -ac 2`) for `.mp3`, `.ogg`, `.flac` and `.m4a`; a headless step carries the fraction of a frame, so a recorded length is within a few frames of the time; tests that start ffmpeg's stand-in are in `roci/movies`. **File formats** (`vendor/sound/c/`): miniaudio's decoder is given backends of our own (`roci_backends` in `roci_sound.c`, tried first, each refusing a file that is not its kind so the next gets its turn): AIFF (`roci_aiff.h`), Opus (`roci_opus.h`, libopus beside it in the same archive from `opus_files.txt`) and AAC by system (`roci_apple.h`, `roci_android.h` which needs API level 28, `roci_windows.h`, whose bare ADTS streams seek by going back to the start, because Media Foundation stamps the first sample after a seek with the time asked for rather than where it really starts, and which calls `IStream`'s table by hand, because miniaudio included `windows.h` before `COBJMACROS`; `roci_sound.c` undefines stb_vorbis's leftover `L`, `C` and `R` macros, which broke `mfapi.h`, and on Windows streams through miniaudio's file system with its own opener, `CreateFileW` sharing reading, writing and deletion, because miniaudio's shares only reading, so a streamed song could not be saved again, and reads the path in the ANSI code page; Ogg Vorbis, whose length stb_vorbis cannot give through a file system, is read whole by the same opener and streamed from its bytes); `roci_aac.h` says what looks like AAC, and where no decoder takes it `vendor/sound/ffmpeg.odin` asks the user's ffmpeg (tests with a stand-in script are in `roci/movies`). `build/sound.odin` makes the library again when any of those files is newer. **SoundFont features** (`roci/audio/soundfont.odin`, `soundfont_mod.odin`): a `Region` keeps the filter, the modulation envelope, the LFOs and its `mods`; `font_control` in `synth.odin` works the pitch, LFOs and filter coefficients every `CONTROL_FRAMES` (32) frames; `tools/soundfont modulated` writes `tests/sounds/modulated.sf2`, the font the filter, LFO and modulator tests read, and a test fails when it is stale. **MIDI out** (`roci/midi_out.odin`, `roci/platform/midi*.odin`, `roci/interp/midiout.odin`): `Midi_Out` sends short messages to the device chosen by part of its name (`midi to`), CoreMIDI destinations, WinMM output devices (reopened when their number changes) and the same raw device files opened for writing on Linux, FreeBSD and OpenBSD, opened by the first send so a sketch that never sends opens nothing; a notes with `for` is let go by the frames, a clock is a thread of its own with a precise sleep, headless sketches keep what they would send (`midi_sent_messages`), and an incoming clock becomes `midi tempo`; the parser keeps pitch bend, program change, pressure and the real-time bytes without touching running status, and WinMM's packed messages are read by the length their status says.
- `vendor/curl` and fetching (`roci/platform/fetch.odin`, `roci/fetch.odin`, `roci/abi_fetch.odin`, `roci/interp/fetch.odin`; §5.19): `vendor/curl` is Odin's own `vendor:curl` (curl 8.23 headers, its LICENSE beside) with the foreign import changed to the static libraries `tools/net-libs.sh` builds into `.cache/net` (libcurl and mbedTLS, HTTP and HTTPS and web sockets only; Schannel on Windows). `platform.fetch_ask` starts a thread (`thread.create_and_start_with_poly_data`, self cleaning) that fills a locked, program-wide table (`fetch_table`, a request is `Waiting`, `Done` or `Failed`); never empty it whole, since sketches on several threads share it (a test that did made another test's request vanish): `fetch_take` and `fetch_forget` take one number. A thread writes into its own job and calls `fetch_deliver`, which drops the answer when the request was forgotten. Headless sketches never start a thread (`roci.ask_fetch` makes a waiting request), so the tests answer with `fetch_deliver`. The C functions keep a finished answer in `fetched` until `roci_fetch_forget`. Trap: curl's cmake has no `HTTP_ONLY` or `ENABLE_WEBSOCKETS` (those are its configure script's `--enable-http-only` and `--enable-websockets`), and an unknown `-D` only warns, so until 2026-11-23 every protocol curl has was built; `tools/net-libs.sh` now switches each other protocol off by its own `CURL_DISABLE_<PROTOCOL>` (web sockets are on by default, SMB needs NTLM and SCP and SFTP libssh, both off), and a new curl release must be checked for new protocols in its `curl_add_if` list. The build's protocols are in `.cache/net/lib/pkgconfig/libcurl.pc` (`supported_protocols`): `HTTP HTTPS WS WSS`.
- `roci/osc` (pure, no sockets): reads and writes Open Sound Control packets (`parse` flattens bundles, `encode` and `encode_bundle`, `match` for handler addresses with `*`, `?`, `[a-z]`, `[!ab]` and `{one,two}`); numbers, text, true/false and blobs (`Blob`) are kept, timetags, MIDI and color arguments are counted in `Message.dropped` so they are said once; every length is checked against what is left, and a test damages good packets 3,000 ways.
  - **OSC in a sketch** (`roci/osc.odin`, `roci/platform/osc.odin`, `roci/interp/osc.odin`): a thread reads a bound non-blocking UDP socket into a locked queue (1,024 packets, the newest kept; `Osc_In`), as MIDI does, and `osc_step` (from `step`) turns the packets into `Osc` happenings against the focused window or the first, so each `on osc` runs once; there is no early hook and no pacing. A handler's address may hold `*` and `?`; a message's strings and numbers are copied into the sketch's own memory and freed with the next frame's messages, because a test reads the log after `step` has let go of the frame's memory.
