Headless sketches and tests open no socket: `feed_osc` gives packets, `osc_sent_messages` is what was sent, `set_osc_port_busy` makes every port taken, and a host ending in `.invalid` is unknown. A port that is taken is `Osc_Port_Busy` and the sketch goes on. `osc send` reaches C as `roci_osc_send` with `roci_value`s; `roci_on` takes "osc" and an address filter.
- Webcam (`roci/webcam.odin`, `roci/platform/camera.odin`, `roci/interp/webcam.odin`; §4.11): `webcam` is a built-in value of kind Image, so every picture verb and every `fill` takes it with no new syntax. `platform.open_camera` opens the first camera SDL lists, at the supported format nearest 640 by 480 (so the device converts as little as it can), and `camera_frame` drains the frames that waited, keeps the newest, converts it with `SDL_ConvertSurface` to RGBA and gives every other one back at once (a frame not released stops the camera). Nothing waits for the person's answer to the system's permission question: until it is `Ready` there is no frame, and `Denied` and `None` (no camera, or it would not open) are said once by the interpreter as the warning `Webcam_Unavailable`. `webcam()` polls the camera once for each frame (`webcam_polled`, set to 0 at the start of every `step`, so set-up and the first `draw` are two looks), makes each new frame a picture of its own (`image_add_pixels`), and gives the same one for the rest of the frame; a picture kept in a variable is a snapshot, and frames not drawn or read for `WEBCAM_KEEP_FRAMES` (90) frames are let go, the newest never. Until a frame has come, with no camera, and in every headless run (which opens none, so goldens and `roci test` are deterministic) it is a 640 by 480 picture of gray 64. Tests feed it with `feed_webcam` and make a problem with `set_webcam_problem`, after `start_headless`, which stops the webcam (so a test that feeds frames cannot use `run_text`). A new GPU texture is made for every frame that is drawn, which costs a 1.2 MB upload at 640 by 480; a texture that is updated in place is the next step if the measurements ask for it. Checked by hand on 2026-11-19 on a Mac with two cameras (kitty allowed in Privacy and Security, the second camera picked in the example's drop-down); Linux, FreeBSD, OpenBSD, Windows and the phones are untried.
- Compiler, first slice (`roci/codegen`, `roci/rt`, `roci/builds`, `cli/compile.odin`, `roci/stats.odin`; built 2026-11-19, §4.7): `codegen.generate` turns the typed IR (after the optimisation passes, as the interpreter runs it) into the Odin source of one `package sketch`: record shapes are structs and records are pointers (shared, as in the language), lists are `^[dynamic]T` (shared too, so a verb that adds to a list it was given changes the caller's), numbers are `f64`, vectors arrays, colours `maths.Colour`, text `string`; every sketch-wide name is a package variable (the checker already makes every assigned name sketch-wide, outside taught verbs); each taught verb instance is a procedure (`result__` is the named result of one that gives, `stop` is `return`); `draw`, `every` and `on` bodies are procedures registered where the sketch wrote them, and `every` and `on key|mouse down|mouse up` run from one frame hook (`hook`) before the `draw` block as the interpreter's do; `keep`, `skip` and `collect` are a procedure made for the call, with a plain loop; verbs are written as the calls of `package roci` the interpreter makes (`interp/verbs.odin`, one `case` of `verb_call` each); a sum of number literals is worked out in the generator with the interpreter's f64 operations because Odin's constants are exact (`0.1 + 0.2` is `0.3` there); division, remainder and power go through `rt` unless the divisor is a nonzero literal (a division by zero is 0). Text made while a frame runs is copied with `rt.keep` where it is stored. Beside the verbs written by hand in `verbs.odin` and `more.odin` (the camera, `light`, `material`, the words that name an enumeration, the transforms in space), `table.odin` holds the ones that are one call with the arguments in order (the solids, `arrow`, `grid`, `axes`, `arc`, `quad`, `clip`, the turtle's moves), checked against the kinds of their arguments, and a window with words after it, `with` styles and `push` are written where they stand. Anything outside the slice (pictures, fonts, sounds, tables, `scene`, `ui`, windows as values, gradients, tweens, `ease`, `bind`, `held`, `learn`'s blocks passed as values, `on` for a window or a message, and the verbs not written here) is a `Problem` with its line, and `generate` returns no source: a sketch is compiled whole or not at all. `roci/rt` is what generated programs import (arithmetic, `format_number` which the interpreter calls too, lists, and `read_run`/`execute`, which take `--frames --seed --screenshot --stats --no-vsync --log --mode`). `roci run --compile` writes `artifacts/<system>-<arch>/<release|debug>/source/sketch.odin`, builds it with `odin build -collection:rocinante=<source of rocinante>` (`-o:speed`, or `-debug -o:none`; the release build of the core takes about 17 s, a debug one 1.5 s), keeps the output in `build.log` and runs the program in the sketch's folder (so `assets/` is found where it is). It needs the pinned Odin on the PATH and the source tree (`ROCI_SOURCE`, or the folder above the one `roci` is in); fetching Odin and the other toolchains is `roci package`'s job (Phase 8). `roci/stats.odin` is shared with the interpreter: frame times from the end of one frame to the next (the first frame is not timed), the box (black, white overlay-font text, 8 pixels from the top left corner, drawn after saves and recordings), a summary with the rate of the slowest hundredth, and `log.json` (an array, written beside and renamed so a killed run leaves the old file whole). `roci/builds` (its own package because it starts processes) compiles every `tests/compile/*.roci` and compares what the program prints with the interpreter's, line by line, and runs each program with `--check-memory` (`--frames` only: every allocation goes through Odin's tracking allocator, the program gives back what its names hold (`rt.at_end` procedures, the collector's objects, the scenes) and says each allocation still not given back with the line that made it and each bad free, and stops with 4; found 2026-11-26, #93, that `print` made its text in the program's heap and never freed it); the golden harness (`tests/golden/compiled.odin`) builds seven examples as programs (`COMPILED`: circle, shapes, particles, life, shapes3d, solids, lamp), runs them with `--frames` and `--screenshot` and judges the pictures against the interpreter's references, so what the compiler covers cannot shrink unnoticed. **Order of evaluation**: Odin does not promise the order in which the arguments of a call or the two sides of an operator are worked out, and the interpreter works them out left to right, which matters for `hsb (random 255) 255 (random from 40 to 255)`. Where two or more of the operands of a call, an operator or a literal are impure (a random number, a call of a taught verb) the generator works them out into names on lines of their own, in order (`in_order`), except where the expression is evaluated more than once or not at all (a loop's test, the right side of `and`, the block of a list verb); each expression is written once (`written`), because writing it twice would work it out twice. `give` sets the result and returns, as the interpreter's `Give` flow ends the verb, and a record written for a collision shape carries the class it was written with (`class__`), since a list that mixes lines, circles and rectangles has one type with every field. Two bugs the examples found: `sengen` (the random numbers in a different order) and `rays` (a list of mixed shapes). **Coverage** on 2026-11-19: 91 of the 92 examples compile, and all 55 draw byte-identical pictures to the interpreter's (`COMPILED` in the golden harness holds sixteen of them, which are built on every full run); the comparison found a bug of the interpreter's too (a movie used as a fill showed its first picture, not the picture of the moment); every one of the 92 now compiles (scenes, messages, the events of MIDI, OSC, UDP, TCP and web sockets, `ui`, data files, `fetch` and dialogs, named styles, `mesh`, 3D collision and mixed values were added after the first slice). Also done since the first slice: pictures, sounds, fonts and movies (`rt.load_image`, `rt.load_sound`, `rt.font`; assets are found in the `assets` of the folder the program runs in), picture and gradient paints, the pixel verbs and canvases, windows as values and `draw`, `on` and `set` for them, `effect` and `shade`, `tween`, `ease`, tables, `sort`, list blocks that read a verb's own names, and the player's controls. **Memory** (`roci/rt/gc.odin`): every record, list and text a sketch keeps is an object of one heap, with a header that links it to the rest (`using gc__: rt.Header` is the first field of a record struct, `rt.List(T)` is a header and its items), a `trace` that marks what it holds and a `release` that frees it. A mark-and-sweep collector runs from the end-of-frame hook, when every verb has finished, so the only places a value can still be held are the sketch's own names: the program writes `roots`, which marks each name that can hold a heap object, and the generator writes a `trace_Record_N` for each record shape and a `mark_item_N` for each kind of list item that holds something. It starts when the objects made since the last collection come to the larger of 8 MB and what survived it, so a sketch that makes little is never stopped. What a frame makes only to use at once (the text of a sum) is in `context.temp_allocator`, which `roci.step` frees; a text stored in a name goes through `rt.keep`, which puts it in the heap. `particles`, which drops and makes hundreds of records a frame, went from 515 MB after 3,000 frames to 31 MB (50 collections freed 401 MB), at the same frame time, and `roci/builds` checks that a compiled sketch that makes garbage holds a bounded amount. **Known gaps**: there is no overlay for runtime errors or limit on how long a block runs (a compiled sketch does what the interpreter does after its error: 0 or empty, and carries on, without saying so), a field read of a record that is not there is empty and a write to it changes nothing (`rt.live`), `is` on lists, records and tables compares what they hold as the interpreter does (`eq_N`, by recursion, so a chain a hundred thousand records long would overflow the stack where the interpreter does not), and a `fetch` or `ask` block that reads a name made inside a taught verb is refused (the interpreter runs the block when the verb is long gone). Measured on a Mac (Apple M4 Pro, headless, so the CPU's share of the frame): `benchmark` 18.2 ms a frame interpreted and 5.2 compiled, `particles` 4.9 and 1.2. **Mixed values** (2026-11-19): where the checker could not settle one kind (a list of numbers, text and true, `osc.args`, and the places a record's field or a verb's value has a mix because two uses of a name disagreed) the program holds an `rt.Box` (`roci/rt/box.odin`, a union of every kind a value can be, with a record, list or table kept with its Odin type and, for a copy or a printing, the procedures the generator wrote for it); `codegen/boxes.odin` runs over the whole program before it is written and puts a conversion (`IR_Convert`) wherever a value goes into a box or comes out of one (a store, a field of a record, an item of a list, a verb's value, the operands of an operator, what a registry verb wants of each value), so the code after it sees one kind in each place; an operation on boxes does what the interpreter does on values of any kind (`rt.box_arith`, `rt.box_equal`, `rt.box_part`, a kind that does not fit is 0 or empty). A vector taken out of a box takes the size the checker gives the mix, three when a vector of three is among them, while each value keeps its own, so a verb whose meaning depends on the size looks into the box as the sketch runs: `move` of a boxed vector is `rt.move_box`, which moves the canvas for two numbers and the shapes in space for three or more, as the interpreter does (fixed 2026-11-26: compiled as the move in space, it left every shape drawn after it at the canvas's origin, which WILDVEX showed as freed animals and weapon icons missing from their place once its `plain` drew a shape's own corners moved into place). Two lists of the same items with boxes in one and one kind in the other are copied item by item, which is right for a list made on the spot and refused ("shared between") for a name that holds it; a field of a box that may be a record is refused. **On phones** (2026-11-19): `--target ios|android` generates the program with an entry an app runs (`codegen.Options.target`; `rt.use_home` says where the app's `assets` and its writable folder are, since the desktop's `.` is not a folder an app has), and `cli/compile.odin`'s `put_on_phone` runs `tools/mobile-ios.sh` or `tools/mobile-android.sh` (`phone_system` maps `android-emulator` to the Android one, with `EMULATOR=1`; `device_variable` says which variable `--device` fills), which build, sign, install and start it; the Android script finds or starts an emulator (`use_emulator`) and there is no iOS Simulator target, because SDL's Metal driver refuses its GPU (see CLAUDE.md, the 2026-11-20 decision) (`spikes/mobile` stays the smoke test of the core on a phone; these scripts are what its build scripts became). Prebuilt for the phone by `tools/mobile-sdl.sh` and `spikes/mobile/*/libs.sh`, into `.cache`; the mobile scripts run `libs.sh` every time (its output in the sketch's `artifacts/phone-libs.log`), and it builds a library only when it is missing or its stamp (`tools/stamps.sh`: the tag and settings, or a `cksum` of our own sources) no longer matches, because building only what was missing left a computer that had built them once with a sound library lacking the movie decoder and no libcurl at all (found 2026-11-22). Fetched sources sit in folders named after their tags, which `tools/package-libs.sh` also reads for SDL_ttf. The iPhone's signature comes from the development team Xcode knows, the Android debug key from `output/spike-debug.keystore`; what is untried: an Android phone (the APK builds), several phones at once, the phones' MIDI, microphone, recording and screen readers, a sketch's orientation, and the sketch's `assets` being larger than a phone's free space.
- Packaging for the Mac (`cli/package.odin`, `cli/package_macos.odin`, `cli/package_macos_parts.odin`, `roci/folder/settings.odin`, `roci/rt/app.odin`, `tools/package-libs.sh`; built 2026-11-20, §4.9): `roci package <target>` checks the target against `DOCTOR_TARGETS` and the host (the same words as `roci doctor`) before anything runs, and only `macos` is written. `package_macos` makes `output/package/macos` of the sketch's folder fresh each time: the settings (`resolve_settings`: flag, file, default), the libraries (`tools/package-libs.sh macos` when `.cache/package/macos/lib` has no `libSDL3.0.dylib` and `libSDL3_ttf.0.dylib`), the program (`make_source` with `codegen.Options{target = "macos", app_id}`, the generator `compile.odin` shares with `--compile`, then `build` with `-minimum-os-version` and `-Wl,-rpath,@executable_path/../Frameworks`), the bundle (`Contents/MacOS/<slug>`, `Frameworks`, `Resources/assets`, `libs`, the seed files, `Licences.txt` from `docs/packaged-notices.txt` and the font's licence, `AppIcon.icns` from `icons/macos/<slug>.iconset` with `iconutil`, `Info.plist` from `info_plist`), the signature (`codesign` the two libraries, then the app with `entitlements_plist(microphone, camera)`: the hardened runtime on every signature (the ad hoc one too, since 2026-11-23: without it the entitlements decide nothing, and an app that touches the microphone with no entitlement is asked about it or ended by macOS instead of being refused) and a timestamp only with an identity, ad hoc when there is none; the ad hoc signature also carries `com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation`, because under the hardened runtime dyld refuses the libraries the app carries when the program and they have no team in common (`code signature ... not valid for use in process: mapping process and mapped file (non-platform) have different Team IDs`, found 2026-11-23 on the first try, when the packaging's own first run failed), and a Developer ID signs the program and its libraries with one team and needs no such thing; **the microphone and the camera are asked for only by a sketch that uses them**: `codegen.Result.uses` is set as the code that reads `mic` (also as the source of `level`, `bands` and `record`) or `webcam` (also `webcam cameras` and `webcam pick`) is written, `make_source` hands it to the packaging, and `Info.plist` gets `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` and `NSCameraUsageDescription`, and the entitlements `com.apple.security.device.audio-input` and `com.apple.security.device.camera`, only for the one used (WILDVEX never reads either, and its Mac app asked for the microphone because both were always there, issue 72); a `Developer ID Application` identity is looked for in the keychain by `find_developer_id`), a check (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), a first run (`--frames 2`, which finds the assets and the libraries), the zip (`ditto`), and notarisation when a profile is set (`notarytool submit --wait`, `stapler staple`, the zip made again). **Three findings.** (1) Odin puts `-L/usr/local/lib -L/opt/homebrew/lib` before any `-extra-linker-flags`, so a program always links Homebrew's SDL3 and SDL3_ttf, whatever is passed; the program is therefore linked as usual and `install_name_tool -change` points it at the libraries `package-libs.sh` built, which `foreign_dependencies` finds by reading `otool -L` (a dependency outside `/System`, `/usr/lib` and `@` paths with no copy of ours is an error). (2) Homebrew's `libSDL3_ttf` depends on HarfBuzz, glib (LGPL), graphite2, libpng and brotli, so it cannot be carried; `package-libs.sh` builds SDL3_ttf as a dylib with its own vendored FreeType and HarfBuzz inside and checks it needs only SDL3 and the system. (3) The static libraries a program links by path or by name (curl, mbedTLS, the interface, the sound decoders, Homebrew's shaderc and SPIRV-Cross) are built for the macOS of the computer that made them (`minos 26.0` here), so an app promises `macos_minimum` of the building Mac and nothing older; building them for an older target needs them in `package-libs.sh` and their `foreign import` paths to follow. The unit tests cover everything that is text (`cli/package_test.odin`, `roci/folder/settings_test.odin`, `rt.seed_home`, the generator's `macos` entry); the tools themselves are tried by hand (release checklist, "Packaging a Mac app"). **Untried:** a Developer ID signature and notarisation (the only identity on this Mac is an Apple Development one), an app on another Mac, a processor other than arm64, and the interface and screen readers in an app.
- Packaging for Linux (`cli/package_linux_app.odin`, `cli/package_linux_parts.odin`, `cli/package_rpm.odin`, `tools/package-libs.sh linux`, `tools/package-linux-docker.sh`, `tools/ci/package-linux.Dockerfile`, `tools/ci/package-linux-distros.sh` with `tools/ci/package-debian.Containerfile`, `tools/ci/package-fedora.Containerfile` and `tools/ci/install-shaderc.sh`, the `[linux]` part of `roci/folder/settings.odin`; built 2026-11-21; the file is `_app` because Odin reads a `_linux` suffix as "only on Linux"): `roci package linux [folder] [--format tar,deb,rpm,arch,appimage|all]` (also `--maintainer`, `--description`, `--category`, kept in `sketch.toml`; the plain tar by default) makes `output/package/linux` of the sketch's folder fresh each time, on a Linux host only (`can_build`). The settings are asked and kept by `ask_settings`, which the Mac's packaging uses too (it was lifted out of `package_macos`). The program is `make_source` with `codegen.Options{target = "linux"}` (the generator's Mac entry: `rt.enter_app(SDL_GetBasePath(), SDL_GetPrefPath("", id))`, so assets are beside the program and what it writes goes in `~/.local/share/<id>`), built with `-Wl,-rpath,$ORIGIN/lib -Wl,--disable-new-dtags` (an RPATH, not a RUNPATH, so the libraries it carries find the ones they ask for in `lib/` too; Odin passes the flag unquoted, a quoted one reaches the linker with its quotes). **Libraries** (`find_carried`): the program's `NEEDED` libraries from `readelf -d`, those of what it carries in turn, the places from `ldd`; the C library, the loader, libstdc++ and libgcc_s are the computer's (`is_system_library`); anything else must be in `CARRIABLE` (SDL3 and SDL3_ttf, shaderc, SPIRV-Cross, SPIRV-Tools, glslang: licences known to allow shipping) or the packaging stops, so an LGPL library is never carried by accident; SDL3_ttf is the one built by `package-libs.sh linux` (its own FreeType and HarfBuzz and libstdc++ inside, `libSDL3` and the C library its only needs), the others are copies of the build computer's. The Arch package carries none and asks for the Arch packages instead (`arch_depends`). **One folder, five formats:** a folder (program, `lib`, `assets`, `libs`, seed files, `Licences.txt`, Inter's licence, the icon) is made and tried (`--frames 2`), then `tar --owner=0 --group=0 -czf` makes the tar; the `.deb` is `ar rcD` of `debian-binary`, `control.tar.gz` (control file, `md5sums` from Odin's `core:crypto/legacy/md5`) and `data.tar.gz` with `usr/lib/<pkg>`, a `usr/bin` link, the desktop entry, every icon of `icons/freedesktop` (each `hicolor/<size>/apps/<slug>.png` there is, and `scalable/apps/<slug>.svg` from a drawing, `linux_icon_files`; the tar and the AppImage take the largest PNG) and a note for `usr/share/doc`, with `Depends: libc6 (>= <the newest GLIBC_ symbol of the program and what it carries>), libstdc++6, libgcc-s1`; the RPM (added 2026-11-22) is written byte by byte by `rpm_package` (RPM's version 4 format as rpm 4.20.1's `lib/header.c`, `lib/signature.c`, `build/pack.c` and `lib/cpio.c` write it: a 96-byte lead, a signature header of the header's SHA-256 and SHA-1, the MD5 of header and payload and the sizes, padded to 8, then the header with its immutable region, every entry in tag order on its alignment and the region trailer last, then a `newc` cpio of the same tree as the `.deb`, files sorted by path and named `./usr/...`, compressed with `zstd -19`), with the file list (SHA-256 digests), `Requires` of `libc.so.6(GLIBC_<newest>)(64bit)`, `libstdc++.so.6(GLIBCXX_3.4.<newest>)(64bit)` and the four `rpmlib()` features it uses, and `Provides` of its name and `name(x86-64)`; it lists as its own only the folders under `/usr/lib/<pkg>` and `/usr/share/doc/<pkg>`, the rest belonging to Fedora's `filesystem`; it is not signed, and rpm 6.1 (Fedora Rawhide) refuses an unsigned package with `rpm -i` (`no signature`), where `dnf install ./x.rpm` installs it with a warning; the Arch package is `tar --zstd` of `.PKGINFO` and the same tree (installs with `pacman -U`); the AppImage is `AppDir` (`AppRun`, desktop entry, icon, `usr/lib/<slug>`) squashed with `mksquashfs -comp zstd` behind the runtime. **The app's id names its windows:** the generated `main` gives SDL the id (`SDL_PROP_APP_METADATA_IDENTIFIER_STRING`) before anything starts it, and the desktop entry's `StartupWMClass` is the id too, because a Wayland compositor matches a window to its desktop file (`<id>.desktop`) and icon by the window's app id, which SDL otherwise takes from the program's name (found 2026-11-21 under niri, where the window said `voyage`); the app's name from `sketch.toml` goes to SDL too (`SDL_PROP_APP_METADATA_NAME_STRING`), so volume controls say "Rain" and not the program's `rain` (seen in PipeWire's `application.name`). No distribution tool is used (no `dpkg`, `rpmbuild`, `makepkg` or `appimagetool`), so any format is made on any Linux. **The runtime** is `type2-runtime` release 20251108, fetched on first use into `.cache/package/linux` and checked against sha256 sums kept in the source; it is MIT with musl, libfuse (LGPL-2.1), squashfuse, zstd and zlib linked in, which was put to Fabrizio and approved on 2026-11-21 on condition of a note inside the AppImage (`AppImage-runtime-licences.txt`) naming libfuse and its source. **Portability:** a program asks for the newest glibc and libstdc++ symbols of the computer that built it (an Arch of 2026-11 asks for glibc 2.44), and the command says so; packages for older computers have to be made on an older Linux. **Tried** (2026-11-21, in the `tools/ci/package-linux.Dockerfile` image under emulation, x86-64 Arch, from an Apple silicon Mac): the tar unpacked and run for two frames on a fresh Arch (the carried libraries found through `lib/`), the Arch package installed with `pacman -U` with its dependencies pulled in, `pacman -Qk` clean and `/usr/bin/<slug>` run, the `.deb` read and installed by `dpkg` on Ubuntu (`md5sums` checked by `dpkg -V`; its libc6 dependency refused there, rightly), the AppImage's squashfs read with `unsquashfs` at the runtime's end and its `AppRun` run. **Tried on a Linux desktop** (2026-11-21, CachyOS, niri on Wayland, `examples/voyage` in all four formats): the tar's program found its seven carried libraries in `lib/` and opened its window, drew the scene lit, shadowed and with its textured model, and wrote the same picture with `--frames --screenshot`; the Arch package's program (unpacked, not installed, so with the system's SDL3 and SDL3_ttf) and the `.deb`'s drew the same picture; `pacman -Qip` read the Arch package and `desktop-file-validate` passed its entry; the AppImage's own runtime mounted it with FUSE and the window opened, with the app id `org.rocinante.voyage`; an AppImage of `examples/sound` played its rain through PipeWire (a stream that was not paused). Those runs drew on llvmpipe, because that computer's NVIDIA driver could not be reached until a reboot (its kernel module was older than its library); after it, all four formats of `examples/voyage` chose the RTX 2080 Ti through Vulkan, held the monitor's 100 Hz (`--stats`) and drew the scene in their windows, and the tar's program without vsync made 1,763 frames a second (0.57 ms a frame). The Arch package was installed there with `pacman -U` (`pacman -Qk` found every file), Voyage showed in the launcher with its icon, and `/usr/bin/voyage` opened a window with the app id `org.rocinante.voyage`. **The RPM, tried** (2026-11-22, `examples/circle` made on CachyOS, in Podman's `fedora:43` and `fedora:rawhide`): `rpm -K -v` says the header and payload SHA-256 digests are OK on rpm 6.0.2 and 6.1.0, `rpm -qip`, `--requires`, `--provides` and `-qlvp` read it as written; on Rawhide (glibc 2.44.9000) `dnf install` installed it, `rpm -V` was clean, `/usr/bin/circle --frames 2` ran and `rpm -e` took every file away; Fedora 43 (glibc 2.42) refused it for `libc.so.6(GLIBC_2.44)(64bit)`, rightly. **Made on older distributions** (2026-11-22, WILDVEX in Podman containers, each with SDL 3.4.18 and SDL_ttf 3.2.2 and SPIRV-Cross built from source): Debian 12 made the `.deb` and the AppImage (glibc 2.35 or newer, GCC 11's libstdc++), Ubuntu 24.04 a `.deb` (glibc 2.39), Fedora 43 the RPM (glibc 2.39); each installed with `apt` or `dnf`, verified with `dpkg -V` or `rpm -V`, ran two frames and was removed on Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.10 and Fedora 45, and the AppImage ran on all three; on the Fedora 45 desktop (a VM, upgraded from 43 with the RPM installed) WILDVEX showed in the app menu with its icon and its window had the icon too, and the `.deb`s worked on the Debian 13 and Ubuntu 26.10 desktops (VMs). `tools/ci/package-linux-distros.sh <sketch>` makes these again, and since 2026-11-22 the Arch package too (`arch`, in `tools/ci/package-linux.Dockerfile`'s image, whose base is Arch's `latest`, so it stays as built until the image is removed: an older image only asks for an older glibc; WILDVEX's installed with `pacman -U` in a fresh Arch container, pulling in `sdl3`, `sdl3_ttf`, `shaderc`, `spirv-cross`, `spirv-tools` and `glslang`, ran two frames and was removed; `pacman -Qkk` says "no mtree file", since the package has no `.MTREE`, while `pacman -Qk` checks it): an image per distribution (tagged with a sum of its Containerfile, the install scripts and `ODIN_VERSION`, so a change builds a new one), a copy of the repository and of the sketch for each in `~/.cache/rocinante-distros`, the three run side by side, the packages copied to the sketch's `output/package/linux-<name>` and the raised build number back into its `sketch.toml`. What it took: Debian 12's own `libshaderc.so.1` (2023.2) leaves glslang's symbols undefined without naming a library for them, so shaderc v2026.4 was built from source there (`libshaderc_shared.so.1`, glslang and SPIRV-Tools inside); Ubuntu 24.04's `libshaderc.so.1` is whole and is carried under that name (`CARRIABLE`), linked through a `libshaderc_shared.so` link to it; Fedora's carries its `libSPIRV-Tools` too; Fedora needs `libstdc++-static` for SDL3_ttf, and cmake there installs into `lib64`, so `tools/net-libs.sh` names `lib`. **Untried:** an RPM on openSUSE (`zypper`), another GPU (AMD, Intel), `dpkg -i` on a desktop, an arm64 Linux, an older distribution (Debian and Ubuntu's own `libc6` and `libstdc++6` against a program built on a new Arch), X11, and the menu on desktops other than niri (GNOME, KDE).
- Packaging for FreeBSD (`cli/package_freebsd_app.odin`, `cli/package_freebsd_parts.odin`, the `[freebsd]` part of `roci/folder/settings.odin`, codegen's `freebsd` target; built 2026-11-22; the files are `_app` and `_parts` because Odin reads a `_freebsd` suffix as "only on FreeBSD"): `roci package freebsd [folder] [--format pkg,tar|all]` (both by default; the format is kept in `sketch.toml`'s `[freebsd]`, apart from Linux's `formats`, and `resolve_settings` sends `--format` to the target's own key; the maintainer, description and category are Linux's keys, shared) makes `output/package/freebsd` of the sketch's folder fresh each time, on a FreeBSD host only. The program is `make_source` with `codegen.Options{target = "freebsd"}` (the same `main` as the Mac and Linux, `rt.enter_app(SDL_GetBasePath(), SDL_GetPrefPath("", id))`, so assets are beside the program and what it writes goes in `~/.local/share/<id>`) built as `--compile` builds it, **with nothing carried**: it links the computer's `libSDL3`, `libSDL3_ttf`, `libshaderc_shared` and `libspirv-cross-c-shared` from `/usr/local/lib`. `find_pkg_deps` reads the program's `NEEDED` libraries (`readelf -d`, `needed_libraries`), resolves them with `ldd` and sorts them (`packaged_libraries`): under `/lib` or `/usr/lib` they are the base system's and left out, under `/usr/local` a package owns them (`pkg which -q`, then `pkg query '%n|%o|%v'`), anywhere else stops the packaging; those packages are the `.pkg`'s dependencies and the tar's `README.txt`. Why nothing is carried: the port's `sdl3_ttf` depends on HarfBuzz and FreeType from other packages, which is how FreeBSD works, where the Linux packages avoid an LGPL glib; a self-contained tar would need its own SDL3_ttf build (`package-libs.sh freebsd`) and was not asked for. **The `.pkg`** is written by `make_freebsd_pkg` in the format `pkg create` writes (pkg 2.9.2, read from a package it made): a zstd tar of `+COMPACT_MANIFEST`, `+MANIFEST` and then the members with an absolute path (`/usr/local/...`, which `tar -P` and `-s ',^usr/,/usr/,'` write), in no-recursion mode from a member list, each owned folder before its contents. The manifest is one JSON object (`pkg_manifest`: name, origin `graphics/<name>`, version, comment cut to 79 letters, maintainer, `www` (rocinante's site), `abi` from `pkg config ABI`, `arch` as `freebsd:15:x86:64`, prefix, flatsize, desc, categories, `deps`, then `files` and `directories` for the full one only); a file's `sum` is `pkg_sum`: `2$` and the BLAKE2b-512 of its bytes (a link's of its target text) in pkg's base 32, a letter for each five bits from the low end of the bytes first, alphabet `ybndrfg8ejkmcpqxot1uwisza345h769`, checked against a package `pkg create` made. Only the app's own folders (`libexec/<name>`, `share/doc/<name>`) are listed as directories, as in the RPM; the rest belong to the system. The tree is `libexec/<name>` (the app's folder), `bin/<name>` (a link into it), `share/applications/<id>.desktop`, `share/icons/hicolor` (the Linux icon set) and `share/doc/<name>/copyright`. After it is made, `pkg info -F` reads it back. The tar is the app's folder with `README.txt`, owned by `root:wheel` (`bsdtar`'s `--uid`, `--uname`, not GNU's `--owner`). **Tried** (2026-11-22, FreeBSD 15.1 amd64, `examples/circle`): both files made, `pkg info -F` reads the package, the unpacked tree's `bin/` link started the program with `--frames 2 --screenshot` and wrote the picture (so `SDL_GetBasePath` resolves the link); then Fabrizio installed the package with `pkg add` on his FreeBSD Wayland desktop and reported that it works, the icons and the command included. **Untried:** `pkg delete` and `pkg check` after it, the tar on a computer with nothing of rocinante, X11, another release or arm64, a repository (`pkg repo`) built from the package.
- Packaging for OpenBSD (`cli/package_openbsd_app.odin`, `cli/package_openbsd_parts.odin`, the `[openbsd]` part of `roci/folder/settings.odin`, codegen's `openbsd` target, `tools/package-libs.sh openbsd`; built 2026-11-23; the files are `_app` and `_parts` for the reason FreeBSD's are): `roci package openbsd [folder] [--format pkg,tar|all]` (both by default; the format is kept in `sketch.toml`'s `[openbsd]`, apart from the other systems', and `resolve_settings` sends `--format` to the target's own key; `tgz` is refused as a word for the tar because OpenBSD's own packages end in it) makes `output/package/openbsd` of the sketch's folder fresh each time, on an OpenBSD host only. The flow is the FreeBSD one with one library carried: the icons first; `package-libs.sh openbsd` once (SDL_ttf `release-3.2.2` built with `-DSDLTTF_VENDORED=ON`, so FreeType and HarfBuzz are inside it, against the installed SDL3, and checked to need only SDL3 and the base system's libraries); the program built with `-extra-linker-flags:-Wl,-rpath,$ORIGIN/lib`, which leaves a RUNPATH that OpenBSD's loader expands (tested with a small C library: it still found `lib` after the folder was moved and from another working folder, and an absolute path did not survive the move); `find_openbsd_needs` reads the program's `NEEDED` libraries (`readelf -d`, `needed_libraries`), resolves them with `ldd` (`parse_openbsd_ldd`: OpenBSD's lines are columns with the path last, not `name => path`), leaves out the carried `libSDL3_ttf.so.0`, and sorts the rest with `library_home`: under `/usr/lib` or `/usr/X11R6/lib` they are the base system's and only become a `-W` library number, under `/usr/local/lib` a package owns them (`pkg_info -E <file>`, then `pkg_info -f <package>` for the line `@comment pkgpath=devel/sdl3`), anywhere else stops the packaging. `fill_stage` makes the folder (the program, `lib/libSDL3_ttf.so.0`, assets, libs, the starting files, the notices), and the app is run once with `--frames 2`. **The `.tgz`** is made by `make_openbsd_pkg`: a tree under `pkg/root/usr/local` (the app's folder in `libexec/<pkg>`, a command in `bin`, the desktop entry, the icons, a note about the licences), a packing list from `openbsd_plist` (`@bin` for the program, `@so` for the library it keeps, a folder with a slash at its end, everything else a plain file), and `pkg_create -x -A <arch> -D COMMENT=<60 letters> -D FULLPKGPATH=graphics/<pkg> -D HOMEPAGE=<rocinante's site> [-D MAINTAINER=<...>] -d <description> -f <list> -B <root> -p /usr/local -P <pkgpath>:<stem>-*:<name> ... -W <name.major.minor> ...`, read back with `pkg_info -f -D unsigned` (`-D unsigned` is what lets `pkg_info` and `pkg_add` open a package with no signature). The command in `bin` is a short script that `exec`s the program by its full path (`openbsd_command`), not a link, so the program never takes the folder of a link for its own. `pkg_create` writes the checksums, sizes and timestamps itself. The tar is `tar -czf` (OpenBSD's `tar` has no long options and no way to say who owns the files, which stay the packager's) of the same folder with a `README.txt` that names the packages to add. **Why `pkg_create` and not our own bytes** as for FreeBSD: `pkg_add` is strict about a package's list and its hashes, `pkg_create` also checks the libraries the program asks for against the dependencies, and it is in the base system of every OpenBSD that can run `roci package openbsd`. **Tried** (2026-11-23, OpenBSD 8.0 amd64, `examples/circle`, and `examples/bricks` as `bricks-game` 2.1.3 with its sounds under `assets`, whose `pkg_add -n -D unsigned` also says ok): both files made (9.8 MB each, SDL3_ttf built in 1 minute 57), `pkg_info -L` lists what is expected, `pkg_add -n -D unsigned` says `circle-1.0.0: ok`, and the unpacked tar's program ran with `--frames 2` from another folder and loaded `lib/libSDL3_ttf.so.0` from beside it and `libSDL3.so.0.1` from the system. Fabrizio then installed the package as root with `pkg_add -D unsigned ./circle-1.0.0.tgz` on his OpenBSD 8.0 machine and reported that it works (2026-11-23); then WILDVEX (assets, fonts, shaders, a vault) in a window, whose first package reported `ripple.hlsl` and the Aileron fonts as missing from the assets folder: the app had kept the string SDL gives for the program's folder, SDL frees it when a window that fails to start quits SDL before the retry on X11 (a headless run never starts a window, so the packaging's own first run did not show it), and OpenBSD reuses freed memory at once; `rt.use_home` keeps a copy now and WILDVEX works (Fabrizio, 2026-11-23). The first line the tool printed for it had left out the `./`, and `pkg_add` said `Can't find` for the bare file name (`openbsd_install_command` says it now). **Untried:** `pkg_delete` after it, the menu entry and icons in a desktop, the tar on a computer with nothing of rocinante, another release (a package says only its processor, and `pkg_add` checks the library numbers) and arm64.
- Packaging for Android (`cli/package_android.odin`, `cli/package_android_parts.odin`, `tools/mobile-android.sh`, `tools/mobile/android/SketchActivity.java`, the `orientation` and `[android] category` keys of `roci/folder/settings.odin`, and `run_phone_script` in `cli/compile.odin`; built 2026-11-23, the first target of issue 56): `roci package android [folder]` makes `output/package/android` of the sketch's folder fresh each time, and in it `name-version-android-arm64.apk`: signed, not debuggable, with the sketch's own name, id, version, icon, orientation and category. The flow is the other packages' (settings asked once, icons first, which must have `android/res/mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml`), then `make_source` for the `android` target with `codegen.Uses`, then `android_manifest` writes the manifest and **`tools/mobile-android.sh` does the building, in the package mode the variables set by `package_android` switch on**: `APP_MANIFEST`, `APP_RES`, `APP_PACKAGE`, `APP_OUT`, `APP_APK`, `APP_KEYSTORE`, `APP_KEYPASS` and `NO_INSTALL=1`; the same script puts a test build on a phone, so the compile (`odin build -target:linux_arm64 -subtarget:android`), the Java glue, `d8` and the phone's libraries are shared, and `run_phone_script` (moved out of `put_on_phone`) is the loop that runs it and draws the steps it says for both. In package mode `aapt2 compile --dir res` and `aapt2 link --manifest` make the APK of the manifest and the icons (aapt2, because the adaptive icon of Android 8 and later is its to read), then `aapt add` puts in `classes.dex`, the five libraries and the sketch's files under `assets/sketch/` (`assets/`, `libs/` and `seed/` holding `ui.toml`, `memory.toml`, `vault.bin` and `vault.key`, with a `.sketch` file so the folder is always there), then `zipalign` and `apksigner sign --ks-pass file:` (**the key's password is the keystore's and is given once**: `apksigner` reads a second `file:` password from the next line of the same file, and the file has one, which was the packaging's first failure). **The manifest** (`android_manifest`) names the activity by its whole class (`org.rocinante.sketch.SketchActivity`), so the id is free to be the app's; has `versionCode` the build number and `versionName` the version; `icon` and `roundIcon`; `appCategory` when `[android] category` is one of Android's nine words and `screenOrientation` from `orientation` (`portrait`, `landscape`, or `any` as `unspecified`), both read from developer.android.com's manifest and ApplicationInfo pages (from Android 17 a phone ignores the orientation of an app on a large screen unless it is a game); `RECORD_AUDIO` and `CAMERA` (with their `uses-feature` not required) only for a sketch whose `Uses` say so; and no `debuggable`. **The id** (`android_application_id`): at least two parts, each starting with a letter and holding letters, digits and underscores; a hyphen becomes an underscore and the packaging says so, anything else stops it. **The key** is made once for the id by `keytool` (RSA 2048, valid 10000 days, a password of 32 random letters and digits written with mode 600) in `android_key_folder` (`~/Library/Application Support/rocinante/android` on a Mac, `$XDG_DATA_HOME` or `~/.local/share` `/rocinante/android` elsewhere), outside the sketch's folder, and `roci` says to keep a copy of it, because only it can sign an update; a key of the person's own is not read yet. **The sketch's files** are read from the APK by `SketchActivity.unpackSketchFiles` before SDL starts: into the app's own folder, where the Android `main` looks (`SDL_GetAndroidInternalStoragePath`), `assets` and `libs` deleted and copied again after every install or update (the stamp is the package's `lastUpdateTime`, kept in `.sketch-files`), each starting file only when the folder has none, and nothing at all for a test build, which has nothing under `assets/sketch` because `adb` put its files there. **Tried** (2026-11-23, a Mac host, the Pixel 9): `examples/bricks` as `org.example.rocitest_bricks` 0.3.1 build 5, its APK read with `aapt2 dump badging` and `dump xmltree` (id, versions, label, adaptive icon, portrait, game, no `debuggable`, the five libraries, the four sounds) and `apksigner verify`, installed with `adb install`, started and seen full-screen in portrait with its game in play, then updated over itself (`unpacked the sketch's files: 4 in assets, 0 in libs`, running, no crash), started a second time (nothing unpacked again), and uninstalled; then Fabrizio packaged WILDVEX (all its assets, fonts, shaders and vault), which installed and worked (the first line it printed named a bare `adb`, which Android Studio does not put on the PATH, and `android_adb_name` names it by its place in the SDK now); the first package for an id makes the key and says so; and `roci run --compile --target android` still puts a test build on the phone after the shared loop and the activity changed. **Untried:** the sounds heard on the phone, a Linux or Windows host, another phone or Android version, an update signed with another key (Android refusing it), the unpacking time of a sketch with hundreds of megabytes (WILDVEX), the microphone and the camera on a phone, and Google Play, which asks for an Android App Bundle (`bundletool`, not here yet).
- The Android App Bundle (built 2026-11-23, issue 56; `tools/fetch-bundle-tools.sh`, the `APP_AAB` step of `tools/mobile-android.sh`, `parse_android_formats` and the `[android] formats` key): `roci package android --format aab|all` makes `slug-version-android.aab`. The script links the manifest and the icons again with the `aapt2` Google publishes for bundles (`--proto-format`; the SDK's build-tools one is not the one bundletool supports), puts them with the dex file, the libraries and the sketch's files as the one `base` module, has bundletool 1.18.3 (Apache-2.0, one jar run with the JDK) build and validate the bundle, and signs it with the same release key as the APK using `jarsigner` (`apksigner` cannot sign a bundle). Both downloads are pinned with their SHA-256 and fetched into `.cache/android/tools` the first time (approved by Fabrizio 2026-11-23). Play wants new apps to target API 36 from 2026-08-31 (developer.android.com/google/play/requirements/target-sdk); `TARGET_API_LEVEL` is 36 in `tools/mobile-android.sh` since 2026-11-26 (it was 34; the script already compiled against the newest platform installed, so only the manifest changed, and the app draws edge to edge as Android 15 and later force).
- Packaging for the iPhone (`cli/package_ios.odin`, `cli/package_ios_parts.odin`, `tools/mobile-ios.sh` in its package mode, the `launch-colour` key of `roci/folder/settings.odin`; built 2026-11-23, the second target of issue 56): `roci package ios [folder] [--team id]` makes `output/package/ios` of the sketch's folder on a Mac: the app and `slug-version-ios.ipa`. `roci` writes the `Info.plist` (name, version, build as `CFBundleVersion`, orientations from `orientation`, no status bar, the microphone and camera sentences only for what `codegen.Result.uses` says) and an asset catalog (the 1024-pixel icon of `icons/ios`, and `LaunchBackground`, the colour of `launch-colour`, named by `UILaunchScreen`), and the script, told by `APP_PLIST`, `APP_CATALOG`, `APP_PRODUCT`, `APP_OUT` and `APP_IPA`, compiles the catalog with `actool` (its partial plist is merged into ours with PlistBuddy, which adds the icon names), copies the compiled pictures into the app inside Xcode's build phase (anything added after signing breaks the signature), has Xcode sign it with the development team and zips `Payload/<app>`. The bundle id is the sketch's id (Apple allows letters, digits, hyphens and periods, so `ios_bundle_id` turns an underscore into a hyphen). The signature is a development one: the app runs on the phones it knows and a free account's lasts seven days; a distribution signature, the App Store and TestFlight are not built. Tried on a Mac with a copy of `examples/bricks`: the app has its own id, name, version, build, icon pictures and compiled catalog and is signed by the team (`codesign`); and Fabrizio's WILDVEX installed and worked on the iPhone 13 Pro (2026-11-23).
- The iPhone's App Store build (built 2026-11-23, issue 56; `ios_export_options`, the `APP_STORE` step of `tools/mobile-ios.sh`, the `--app-store` and `--upload` flags): `roci package ios --app-store` has Xcode archive the project and export it with `-exportArchive` and an options plist `roci` writes (method `app-store-connect`, `destination` `export`, or `upload` with `--upload`, automatic signing, `manageAppVersionAndBuildNumber` false so the build number stays `roci`'s, no symbols; the keys are the ones `xcodebuild -help` lists), into `slug-version-ios-appstore.ipa`. Xcode must make the distribution signature and profile from the team's account, so it needs a paid Apple Developer Program team and an app record with the bundle id in App Store Connect. **Untried past that point**: with the free account on this Mac the archive works and the export stops with Xcode's "Team does not have permission to create iOS App Store provisioning profiles", which `roci` explains; the signed `.ipa`, the upload and TestFlight are written from Apple's documentation and `xcodebuild -help` and have never run. `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption` is left out of the `Info.plist` on purpose: the encryption declaration is the developer's to make in App Store Connect.
- Packaging for Windows (`cli/package_windows_app.odin`, `cli/package_windows_parts.odin`, the `[windows]` part of `roci/folder/settings.odin`, codegen's `windows` target, the `windows/msix` logos of `cli/package_icons.odin`; built 2026-11-23, the third target of issue 56; the files are `_app` and `_parts` because Odin reads a `_windows` suffix as "only on Windows"): `roci package windows [folder] [--format zip,msix|all] [--sign certificate]` makes `output/package/windows` of the sketch's folder fresh each time, on a Windows host only; `work` is a whole path with slashes (`absolute_path`, `slashed`), because `os.process_exec` could not start a program by a relative path with slashes there (found on the first run). The generator's `windows` entry is the Mac's `main` (`SDL_GetBasePath` is the folder of the `.exe`, `SDL_GetPrefPath("", id)` is `%APPDATA%\<id>`). The program is built with `-subsystem:windows` (no console; a GUI program is not waited for by PowerShell's `&`, so a hand check uses `Start-Process -Wait`) and `-resource:` a script `windows_rc` writes (UTF-8 through `#pragma code_page(65001)`: `1 ICON` the `.ico` of `icons/windows`, `1 24` the manifest `WINDOWS_MANIFEST` with Windows 10's `supportedOS` and PerMonitorV2, and a `VERSIONINFO` of `windows_version`'s four numbers, each at most 65,535, the build fourth); Odin runs `rc` itself. **Libraries** (`find_windows_libraries`): `pe_imports` reads the import table of the program, then of each DLL it carries (the PE format by hand: `e_lfanew`, the optional header's magic for where the data directories are, the import directory's RVA mapped to the file through the section table, 20-byte descriptors to a zero one); a DLL in `System32` or an API set (`api-ms-win-*`, `ext-ms-*`) is Windows' own, and anything else must be in `WINDOWS_CARRIABLE` (`SDL3.dll` and `SDL3_ttf.dll`, from `odin root`'s `vendor/sdl3`) or the packaging stops; a compiled sketch asks for those two only (checked with `dumpbin /dependents`: Direct3D's `D3DCOMPILER_47.dll`, Media Foundation, UI Automation and the rest are Windows'), and Odin's `SDL3_ttf.dll` has FreeType, HarfBuzz, plutosvg and plutovg inside, named in `docs/packaged-notices.txt`. `roci run --compile` on Windows copies the same two beside its program, and says in words when Windows could not start one for a missing library (`windows_start_failure`, `0xC0000135`). The app's folder (`windows_file_name` of the app's name) holds the program, the DLLs, `assets`, `libs` (without `.git`), the starting files and the notices, copied by Odin (`copy_tree`), not `cp`. **Signing**: `WINDOWS_CERTIFICATES_SCRIPT` asks PowerShell for `Cert:\CurrentUser\My -CodeSigningCert` with a private key and not expired, `pick_windows_certificate` takes the one `--sign` names (a thumbprint, or a part of the subject that is one certificate's) or the only one there is, and `signtool sign /sha1 <thumbprint> /fd SHA256 /tr http://timestamp.digicert.com /td SHA256` signs the program and the DLLs, then the MSIX; `signtool` and `makeappx` are found in the newest `Windows Kits\10\bin\<version>\x64` (`newest_sdk_folder`), since they are on the PATH only inside `vcvars64.bat`. The app is run once (`--frames 2`). The zip is `%SystemRoot%\System32\tar.exe -a -c -f` (Windows' bsdtar; Git Bash's GNU tar may come first on the PATH and writes no zip). **The MSIX**: the app's folder plus `logos` (44, 50 and 150 pixels, made with the icons) and `AppxManifest.xml` from `appx_manifest` (name `msix_name` of the id, publisher the certificate's subject exactly, or `CN=<name>` unsigned, version the four numbers, `runFullTrust`, microphone and webcam only when `codegen.Uses` says), packed by `makeappx pack /h SHA256`; the logos are in `logos`, not `Assets`, because Windows' names ignore case and the sketch's `assets` is already there. **Tried** (2026-11-23, Windows 11 26300, an RTX GPU on Direct3D 12): `examples/circle`, `voyage` and `sound` packaged; each program started from another folder and drew (voyage's frame lit, shadowed and with its textured model), `VersionInfo` showed the name and `1.0.0.1`, and Explorer's icon was the one made; with a self-signed `CN=rocinante test` certificate in the user's store and trusted in `LocalMachine\TrustedPeople`, it was found without `--sign`, `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` said Valid and time-stamped for the program, `SDL3.dll` and the MSIX, and `Add-AppxPackage` installed the MSIX, which showed in the Start menu, ran from `C:\Program Files\WindowsApps` with its window and was removed with `Remove-AppxPackage`. **Untried:** a certificate from an authority and SmartScreen with it, the Microsoft Store (which wants the fourth number of an MSIX to be 0), the zip on a Windows with nothing of rocinante, Windows 10, arm64, and a sketch with a vault and fonts packaged.
- The Windows installer (`roci/install`, `roci/install/setup`, `make_windows_setup` in `cli/package_windows_app.odin`; built 2026-11-23, chosen by Fabrizio over an MSI made with WiX and over provisioning the MSIX for every user): `roci package windows` makes `slug-version-windows-x64-setup.exe` by default with the zip (`--format setup`, also `installer` or `exe`). **The program** is `roci/install/setup` (package `setup`; `setup_other.odin` only says it is a Windows program, so `ci` checks it on every system with `-target:windows_amd64`), compiled at each package with a resource script of the app's icon and version ("<name> Setup") and `WINDOWS_SETUP_MANIFEST`, which asks for `requireAdministrator`; it uses no part of the core, so it builds in seconds and is about 400 KB. **The payload** (`roci/install`, tested everywhere): an archive of our own (`ARCHIVE_MAGIC`, then per file the path, the method, the sizes and the CRC-32 before the bytes; deflated by sancho's `deflate.compress`, stored when that is no smaller or the kind is compressed already, `STORED_KINDS`; read back with `core:compress/deflate` and checked against size and CRC), appended to the program with a trailer (`TRAILER_MAGIC`, the archive's start and length, padded so the file ends on 8 bytes and signtool adds no padding of its own); `find_archive` reads the PE header's certificate table (the fifth data directory, a place in the file) to step past the signature, which signtool appends after everything else, so the archive is inside what the signature covers. `read_archive` refuses a path that could leave the folder (`safe_path`: absolute, a drive, `..`, backslashes, characters Windows refuses). The archive holds `setup.ini` first (`App`: name, id, version, publisher, folder, program, size; `read_ini` refuses a folder or program that is not a plain name), then the app's folder, then `uninstall.exe`: the same program, signed, without the archive. **Installing** (`run_install`): Program Files from `FOLDERID_ProgramFiles` or `/D=`; the version installed from the entry's `DisplayVersion` and `InstallLocation` (`RegGetValueW` with `RRF_SUBKEY_WOW6464KEY`); one `MessageBoxW` question (`ask_install`), none with `/S`; the version before is taken away first, but only a folder holding `setup.ini` is ever emptied, and an existing folder with other files stops it; **a running app is found by opening every file for writing with no sharing** (`in_use`), because Windows lets a running program be renamed (the first try renamed `voyage.exe` while it ran, half removed the folder and failed); then the files, the shortcut in `FOLDERID_CommonPrograms` through `IShellLinkW` and `IPersistFile` (vtables declared by hand in the order of `shobjidl_core.h`, since `core:sys/windows` has none), and the entry under `HKLM\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\<id>` in the 64-bit view (`uninstall_values`: `DisplayName`, `DisplayVersion`, `Publisher`, `DisplayIcon`, `InstallLocation`, `UninstallString`, `QuietUninstallString`, `EstimatedSize`, `NoModify`, `NoRepair`). **Removing**: `uninstall.exe /uninstall` asks (`ask_remove`), copies itself to `%TEMP%\roci-uninstall-<pid>.exe`, starts it with `/remove <folder> /wait <pid>` and ends, since a running program cannot delete its own file; the copy waits for it (`OpenProcess`, `WaitForSingleObject`), refuses a folder without `setup.ini` and `uninstall.exe`, removes the folder, the shortcut and the entry (`RegDeleteKeyExW` with `KEY_WOW64_64KEY`), and has Windows delete itself at the next start (`MoveFileExW` with `MOVEFILE_DELAY_UNTIL_REBOOT`). The whole installer is read into memory, so a sketch of several hundred megabytes needs that much while it installs. **Tried** (2026-11-23, Windows 11, `examples/voyage` signed with the self-signed test certificate): `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` says the installer and its `uninstall.exe` are Valid and time-stamped; `setup.exe /S` (elevated) installed the ten files, the shortcut (target, working folder and description right) and the entry (Publisher the certificate's name, `EstimatedSize` 13,516 KB); the app started from the shortcut as the user, from Program Files, with its window; 1.0.1 installed over 1.0.0 (`DisplayVersion` 1.0.1, the program `1.0.1.2`); installing while the app was open exited 3 and left all ten files; uninstalling while it was open left everything; closed, `uninstall.exe /uninstall /S` removed the folder, the shortcut and the entry, and its copy waits in `%TEMP%` for the next start. **Untried:** the message boxes clicked through by hand, with Narrator, an unsigned installer and SmartScreen, `/D=` to another folder, Settings, Apps starting the uninstall, Windows 10, and a sketch of hundreds of megabytes.
- Packaging icons (`cli/package_icons.odin`, 2026-11-22; `roci/gfx/raster.odin` paints them): `prepare_icons` runs first in `package_macos` and `package_linux`, before anything is compiled, so a picture that cannot be used stops the packaging at once (exit 2). It puts `--icon`'s picture in `icons/` as `icon.svg` or `icon.png` after `read_icon_source` has checked it (a drawing's view box square within a thousandth, a PNG square and at least `ICON_MIN_PNG`, 1024), removing the other one; reads `icons/icon.svg`, else `icon.png`; and makes `icon_files(slug)`, WILDVEX's own layout so its hand-drawn sets are used as they are: `macos/<slug>.iconset` (`.Mac`: the art at 100/1024 in, 824/1024 wide, masked by a rounded square of corner `ICON_CORNER` 0.2237 of the side), `ios/AppIcon-1024.png` (`.Opaque`, over white), `windows/<slug>-<s>.png` for `ICON_WINDOWS_SIZES` and `<slug>.ico` (`ico_encode`, PNG entries, written after its PNGs and from whatever PNGs are there), `freedesktop/hicolor/<s>x<s>/apps/<slug>.png` for `ICON_LINUX_SIZES` and `scalable/apps/<slug>.svg` (the drawing's own bytes; removed when the picture becomes a PNG), and Android's `play-store-512.png`, `mipmap-<density>/ic_launcher.png` (`.Rounded`), `ic_launcher_round.png` (`.Round`), `ic_launcher_background.png` (`.Adaptive_Back`: the art in the middle 72 of 108, `raster_extend` carrying its edges to the border) and `ic_launcher_foreground.png` (empty), with the two `mipmap-anydpi-v26` XML files; no monochrome layer is made, since a picture edge to edge has no silhouette. **What is whose** (`plan_icon`): a missing file is made; a file whose sum `icons/.made` does not hold, or holds another sum for, is the person's own (`.Yours`, listed by `say_icons`, never written); one of roci's own is kept while `.made`'s `source` is the picture's sum and made again when it is not. **No picture**: the rocinante logo (`docs/logo/rocinante-logo.svg`, 1480 by 1080, drawn four fifths wide on a white square, `Icon_Source.contain`) fills what is missing in a copy of the sketch's `icons/` in the package's work folder (`copy_files`, in Odin, since a test may not start a process), so nothing is written into the sketch, and `say_icons` says which were the person's and which the logo's. **Tried** (2026-11-22, CachyOS): a copy of `examples/circle` with an SVG of gradients, a clip, a stroke and an even-odd hole made all 57 files (the Mac's rounded square, Android's circle and adaptive layers looked at), its `.deb` installed every hicolor size and the drawing; a copy of WILDVEX, with its generator's sets and no picture, kept every file of its own (its `.deb`'s 512-pixel icon is byte for byte WILDVEX's) and said the stale `icon` key in its `sketch.toml`; WILDVEX's `icons/master/wildvex.svg` (clip paths) painted like its hand-made 1024-pixel icon. **Untried:** the Mac (`iconutil` from the generated iconset, Finder and the Dock showing it), and any other system's package, which do not exist yet.
- Frame rate (`roci/rate.odin`, `roci/loop.odin` `hold_rate`, `roci/stats.odin` `limit_line`, `roci/abi_window.odin` `roci_set_window_fps` and `roci_window_fps`; built 2026-11-24, §4.5): `Win.fps` is a window's limit (`fps_limit_of`: 0 for none, a negative, endless or NaN number, at least `FPS_LEAST` otherwise) and `frame_limit` the lowest of the open windows'. After each frame `run` calls `hold_rate`, which sleeps until the next frame is due: the deadline moves on by exactly one frame's time from the last deadline (`rate_next_due`), not from when the frame ended, so the average rate is exact even where the refresh is not a whole multiple (60 on 144 Hz lands frames two and three refreshes apart, which `--stats` says in its box and summary, `rate_uneven`); a sketch more than a frame behind starts again from now instead of hurrying. When the first window waits for its screen (vsync) the loop wakes half a refresh before the frame is due (`rate_wake`), so the frame takes the refresh nearest to its deadline, and a screen no more than 1% faster than the limit (`RATE_SLACK`) is left to pace the sketch alone, since sleeping as well would miss refreshes. **On Wayland** (2026-11-26) a window the rate holds below its screen's refresh stops waiting for its screen and hands its frames over in mailbox mode (`pace_windows`, `rate_paces_alone`, `platform.set_paced`; whole frames, no tearing), so the sleep alone paces it, and it waits again once the limit is lifted or a sketch sets `vsync`; a screen with no mailbox (`mailbox_missing`, asked once) keeps waiting. Waiting there made a frame wait for the one before to be shown before it was even built: WILDVEX's demo held at 60 on niri at 99.98 Hz (an RTX 2080 Ti) waited up to 12 ms for its screen, frames ended 7 to 30 ms apart, and the game slowed for a moment now and then; with mailbox no frame waited and the spread was that of X11 and `--no-vsync`, which played smoothly, and Fabrizio watched it play as smoothly as X11 on niri (2026-11-26, together with the fix of the kept triangles, whose forgetting made frames skip on every system that day). X11, Windows and the Mac were smooth waiting and are unchanged. `--stats` still counts refreshes at the screen's rate for a paced window (`first_refresh`'s `shown`). The wait is SDL's (`SDL_DelayNS` in steps of at most a millisecond, running MIDI blocks between them, then `SDL_DelayPrecise` for the last two), because Odin's `time.sleep` on Windows is `Sleep` in whole milliseconds. While a rate holds, the MIDI pacing (`pace_learn`) is not told the frames were late. `--no-vsync` does not lift the limit. A headless `step` with no `dt` takes one frame at the limit (`headless_step`), else a sixtieth, so `time` and `frame` keep the relation they have in a window; a sketch with no `fps` is unchanged. A restart takes the limit away until the sketch asks again (`reset_settings`). Checked by hand by Fabrizio on a Mac with a 60 Hz screen (2026-11-25). Untried by hand: the rate on a 120 or 144 Hz screen of each system, on the phones (ProMotion and the Pixel 9's 120 Hz), and how even `SDL_DelayNS` is on Windows.
- Screens and platform (`roci/screens.odin`, `roci/platform/screens.odin`, `roci/abi_screens.odin`, `roci/interp/touch.odin`; built 2026-11-19): `platform` is a built-in Text value, the operating system's name in lowercase (`macos`, `windows`, `linux`, `freebsd`, `openbsd`, `ios`, `android`), fixed when the program is built (`platform.system_name`: iOS and Android by subtarget, then `ODIN_OS`, anything else is `linux`). `screens` is a built-in List of records (`name`, `pos`, `width`, `height`, `density`, `refresh`, `main`; the checker's `screen_type`, under site -11), the main display first. `platform.screens_now` asks SDL (`GetDisplays`, `GetDisplayBounds`, `GetDesktopDisplayMode`, `GetDisplayContentScale`) with a `VIDEO` init and quit around it, and `screen_from` turns what SDL says into a screen: `width` and `height` are SDL's bounds, which are in the units a window is sized in (points on a Mac, so the real pixels are `width * density`), and where SDL counts in device pixels (Android, `WINDOW_IS_IN_PIXELS`) they are divided by the display scale, as a window's `width` is, so a screen's `width` is a size a window can have. `roci.screens()` asks once a frame at most (a screen plugged in shows at the next frame), and a headless run or a refused graphics start has one stand-in screen of 1920 by 1080 at density 1 and 60 Hz (`HEADLESS_SCREENS`), so golden pictures and `roci test` do not depend on the computer; tests say what the screens are with `set_screens` (after `start_headless`, which stops what was set). `screens` is in the hoisting list of impure words. C has `roci_platform`, `roci_screen_count`, `roci_screen_name`, `roci_screen_pos`, `roci_screen_size`, `roci_screen_density`, `roci_screen_refresh` and `roci_screen_main`. Checked by hand on 2026-11-19 on a Mac with one display (3440 by 1440, density 1, 60 Hz); several screens, a Retina screen, hot-plugging and every other system are untried, and Android's division by the display scale is untested on a phone. `examples/screens` is the showcase of `screens` (its picture holds nothing that differs between systems); `examples/platform` draws a simple mark of plain shapes for each of the seven systems (not their real logos, which belong to their owners), starting on the one the sketch runs on, with the arrow keys going through the others. It has no golden picture and is not on the site (`NO_PICTURE`, `SHOWCASE_LEFT_OUT`), because its picture differs from system to system; an interpreter test presses the keys through every mark. **`safe`** (decided 2026-11-20, issue about the phones): a built-in record `safe` with `top`, `bottom`, `left` and `right`, the strips of the current window that a phone keeps for itself (notch, camera, status bar, home bar, rounded corners), in the window's units; `platform.safe_insets` reads `SDL_GetWindowSafeArea` and `insets_from` turns the rectangle into four insets in logical pixels (Android's window is in pixels, so they are divided by `input_scale`), and `roci.safe_area` gives them, zeros with no window and in a headless run (`set_safe_area` for tests). It is read at each use rather than kept from `SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_SAFE_AREA_CHANGED`, so it follows a phone as it turns with no event to handle, and it is an impure word for the optimiser. The checker's shape is site -12, the interpreter's `safe_record` and the compiler's `builtin_record` read the same procedure, and C has `roci_safe_area` returning `roci_insets`. `platform.safe_inset` (left and top) still serves the interface. On Android the window draws edge to edge (`SketchActivity`), so SDL's `onNativeInsetsChanged` insets, which are the largest of the system bars, the system gesture strips and the cut-out, are what `safe` reads; before that the window stopped at the bars and `safe` was 0. Checked by tests with a pretended area; a phone's real numbers are not checked yet. **The background and `vibrate`** (decided 2026-11-20, `roci/away.odin`, `roci/vibrate.odin`, `roci/platform/vibrate.odin`): `platform.set_foreground_watch` sits beside `set_background_watch` and gets `WILL_ENTER_FOREGROUND` and `DID_ENTER_FOREGROUND`; `away_begin` (called by `background_flush`) and `away_end` (`foreground_resume`) keep two atomics, the moment of leaving and the nanoseconds away before it, so the clock `step` makes is `since(started_at) - away_seconds`; `in_background` makes `run` skip a frame's work and sleep 20 ms. `vibrate` clamps its seconds to 0.01 to 5 and calls `platform.vibrate` (SDL haptics on Android, AudioToolbox on an iPhone, nothing elsewhere); tests read `vibrations()`.
- Movies (`roci/movies.odin`, `roci/abi_movies.odin`, `roci/interp/images.odin`, `vendor/sound/movie.odin` and `c/roci_movie.h`; §4.11): the decoder is pl_mpeg, compiled into the sound decoders' library by including `roci_movie.h` from `roci_sound.c`, so every build path (Unix, Windows, both phones) carries it with no new step; its C functions are `roci_mpeg_*` (the `roci_movie_*` names are the C API's) and the Odin wrapper is `sound.movie_open`, `movie_advance`, `movie_seek`, `movie_picture` and the rest (the bytes of a picture are the decoder's, good until the next call, and pl_mpeg leaves the alpha byte alone, so the wrapper sets it to 255). `roci.add_movie` opens a file's bytes and gives the first picture's number as the movie's handle; a movie is typed as a picture by the checker (a `.mpg` is not special there), so the checker accepts it wherever a picture goes, and `movie_frame(handle)` is the one place that turns it into the picture of the moment: `interp.as_image` calls it, so every picture verb sees the current frame, and anything that is not a movie comes back as itself. `movies_step(seconds)` runs once a frame next to `sounds_step` and advances every movie that plays by `seconds * speed` (the headless step is a sixtieth of a second, so a picture of a movie is the same on every computer); the picture of a frame is made the first time it is asked for (`image_add_pixels`, the previous one let go with `image_release`), so a movie nobody draws costs its decoding only. A restart (`gfx.images_epoch`) forgets every movie. `play`, `loop`, `pause`, `rewind`, `forward` and `set` each have a shape with a picture first (the registry's `MOVIE_OPTIONS` and `MOVIE_SET_OPTIONS`), and a number that is no movie is left alone by all of them. `tests/movies/tiny.mpg` is the 160 by 96, two second test pattern the tests use (made with ffmpeg: `-f lavfi -i testsrc2=size=160x96:rate=25 -t 2 -an -c:v mpeg1video -b:v 200k -g 12 -bf 0 -f mpeg`), and `examples/movie` is the showcase (a Mandelbrot zoom, drawn as a picture and as the fill of a circle and a bar). Not done: the sound of a movie, a movie that is not MPEG-1, recording, and a snapshot of the picture of the moment as a verb of its own.
- `roci/collide`: 2D collision in pure `f64`; shapes become convex pieces of at most four corners plus a radius; `Tree` must answer exactly what checking every shape answers (a test compares them on random shapes).
- `roci/physics` and 2D bodies (`roci/physics.odin`, `roci/physics_link.odin`, `roci/abi_physics.odin`, `roci/interp/physics.odin`, `roci/rt/physics.odin`, `vendor/box2d`; built 2026-11-28): `roci/physics` is the only package that calls Box2D, in pixels and degrees; a `World` owns a `b2WorldId` and a slot table (`Body` is a slot number, 0 for none, a stale number never reaches another body). **`b2CreateWorld` and `b2DestroyWorld` are not thread safe** (a static scan of the world array), so `world_lock` serialises them: without it the parallel tests crashed or hung; worlds themselves are independent, so threads each have their own. The step uses a fixed 4 sub-steps and the frame's seconds clamped to `LONGEST_STEP`, results are rounded to four decimals (`tidy`) because Box2D's 32-bit floats give back 300.0000047 for a 300 that never moved. `roci/physics_link.odin` is the one place that decides what is read before a step and written after it (a field changes only when it moved by more than `PHYSICS_NOISE`), used by both the interpreter (`Body_Link`, fields found by `field_of`, bodies kept through the collector, which moves long records) and compiled sketches (`rt/physics.odin` finds fields by reflection once and keeps pointers; the collector is told to keep the records), so both move a sketch identically (`tests/compile/physics.roci`, `examples/pachinko` byte-identical). `roci.physics_sync(before, after)` is how a program says what to run around the step; `loop.odin` steps before the end hook. At most 20,000 bodies. The iPhone and Android libraries are built by `tools/physics-libs.sh`; `examples/car` compiled ran on the Pixel 9 (2026-11-28); Windows and the iPhone are untried.
- `roci/geom`: plane geometry in pure `f64`, our own tessellation (curves, ear clipping with bridges, `stroke_polyline`, `trace_outline`).
- `roci/gfx`: the drawing model, no GPU. A `Command_List` per window stores 64-byte `Recorded` commands pointing into `looks`; style and transform change only through setters (`look_changed`). Non-finite numbers drop the command, negative sizes are made positive. `Batcher.build` makes instances for one signed-distance "shapes" pipeline; what it cannot draw as styled (`needs_mesh`) is cut into triangles by geom. `command_hash` lets an unchanged batch reuse its GPU buffer.
  - **Text, pictures, fills** all go through the **typeset pipeline** (`typeset.hlsl`, `Typeset_Item`; the shader's `coloured` is a set of flags added to 2: 1 premultiplied, 2 clip what the picture does not reach, 4 repeat). Text is measured when recorded (`measure_typeset`, no GPU) and cached by characters, face, size, alignment and wrap for 120 frames. Faces and fonts live in a thread-local set on the heap allocator; fallback fonts must be the size of the font they serve. A computer without the compiled typeset shader draws everything but text and pictures (`can_typeset`); its DXIL needs `shaders/compile-dxil.sh` after a change to `typeset.hlsl`. Pictures are decoded once by `core:image` into a thread-local store (`images_stop` raises `images_epoch`); a canvas has no CPU pixels, so it cannot feed `outline` (it fills a text by being read back, below).
  - **Kept triangles** (`shapecache.odin`, decided 2026-11-26, #80): the triangles of a fill (`geom.triangulate`) and of an outline (`geom.stroke_polyline`) are made in the shape's own space, so `mesh_add_command`, `arc_into_mesh` and `path_into_mesh` ask `Mesh.shapes` for them (`cut_fill`, `cut_outline`) and only the move onto the canvas (`add_triangulation`) is done each frame. The key is two 64-bit hashes of the corners' bits and, for an outline, its half width, ends, joins, miter limit, tolerance and whether it closes (a tolerance follows the scale, so a shape that keeps growing is cut again each frame when its outline has round joins or ends; one that is only moved or turned is kept); a hit is exactly what a miss would have made, so no picture changed. The shapes are kept in two generations (`Shape_Generation`), each a map and a `mem.Dynamic_Arena` of megabyte blocks holding the corners and indices: new shapes go into the newer one, a shape found in the older one is copied into the newer one, and `shapes_next_build` (at the start of `build_commands`) lets the older one go whole every 60 builds, or when the newer one holds half a million corners, by resetting its arena and clearing its map, which keep their memory for the next generation; so a shape unused for 60 to 120 builds is forgotten, and forgetting costs the same however many shapes there are. **Fixed 2026-11-26**: the first cache freed each forgotten shape on its own, once every 120 builds, and WILDVEX's world canvas (its own batcher, which `--stats`' box and the Pixel 9 count below did not show) makes about 1,300 shapes a frame whose corners change and are never drawn again, so every two seconds it freed about 150,000 shapes in 72 to 74 ms and the game skipped frames on every system, Android and X11 included (found by bisecting with WILDVEX's demo: 2 frames slower than 15 ms in 7,500 before the cache, 88 after); `Stats.shapes_cut` and `shapes_kept` count a build's cut and kept shapes, and `--stats` sums them over the frames it times (the first is left out) into its summary line and the log's `shapes_cut` and `shapes_kept`, saying nothing when no shape went through the mesh; the box has a line of the last build's counts (`shapes 12 cut 340 kept`) for a phone, where nothing is printed. **Measured on the Pixel 9, 2026-11-26** (WILDVEX compiled, the box forced on): its title and its play send only 6 to 10 shapes a frame through the mesh (the rest is circles, rectangles, pictures and masks), so with the cache off it cut 6 to 10 and with it on it kept every one (`shapes 0 cut 6 kept`), and either way it held its limit of 60 fps (58.8 to 60.6): the cache is for sketches that draw many polygons, and does nothing visible for WILDVEX. Measured on this Mac with `-o:speed`, 2,000 polygons of 11 corners, filled and outlined, moved by the processor only: 7 to 11 ms cut every time, 0.9 to 1.4 ms kept; the move onto the canvas on the graphics card, as circles have it, is not done.
  - **Canvases** (`canvas.odin`, `renderer_canvas.odin`): a canvas is drawn after every canvas its rounds show (`list_canvases` reads their Image commands, picture fills and background picture; `Canvas.rendering` ends a loop of two that show each other, where one reads the other's last picture), whatever order they were made in (found 2026-11-26, #82 and #83: a canvas drawn into one made before it was read before it had its picture, and so was one in a sketch with no window once the canvas that shows it held 2,000 commands, which is drawn at once), `examples/canvas_layers`; two textures each, because a multisampled pass cannot add to the texture it resolves into; a round with `background_set` lets earlier rounds go; a canvas texture is premultiplied (typeset mode 3). Density is the window's or `HEADLESS_CANVAS_DENSITY` (2).
  - **Effects** (`translate.odin` shared; `translate_spirv.odin` everywhere but Windows, the only importer of `vendor/shaderc` and `vendor/spirvcross`; `translate_d3d.odin` on Windows; `shaders.odin`, `renderer_effect.odin`): each half gives `translate_fragment`, `user_shader_code` and `can_run_effects`, and `Shader_Data.code` is the system's own form. SPIR-V half: shaderc (HLSL to SPIR-V), SPIRV-Cross to Vulkan GLSL (joins textures to samplers), shaderc again, Metal from that SPIR-V; `place_uniform_blocks` puts `Rocinante` on set 3 binding 0 and the file's globals on binding 1, and numbers Metal buffers itself. Windows half: FXC (`d3dcompiler_47.dll`) makes DXBC 5.1; FXC puts globals on `b0, space0`, where SDL binds nothing, so a shader with globals is compiled twice (`reflect_block`, then `move_globals` blanks the declarations, keeping line numbers, and declares them in `rocinante_values_` on `b1, space3`); messages are rewritten in shaderc's form, but the words differ, so `tests/errors/runtime/shader_broken` has a `.windows.txt`. Built-in effects are HLSL files in `effects/` listed in `BUILTIN_EFFECTS` (a new one is a file, a table row and a guide line); a hash in one must use whole-pixel coordinates so every GPU agrees. `shade` fills use `Style.fill_shader` and `draw_shader_fill`; `interp` keeps a file used both ways as two entries. `prepare_shaders` (called by `render_acquired`, so only for a window's frame) makes, before the frame, the pipelines of every translated shader that has none for the swapchain's format: an effect's, and a fill's at the window's sample count with stencil `Off` and `Attached`; `Effect_Gpu.prepared` keeps which were asked for so a refused one is not asked for every frame. A canvas's pipelines are still made on first use, so its first draw does not wait for every shader.
  - **`mesh`** (`gfx/mesh3.odin`, `interp/meshes.odin`): a list of corners becomes the `Model_Data` of a model kept under the key of the call that draws it (the place of the call in the source plus its number in the frame), and its `edges` are the lines of `as wireframe`, drawn as the cylinders and spheres the space turtle uses; a new place that draws meshes of changing size needs no key of its own, but a key that is used by two calls that draw in turn would send each mesh again every frame.
