The docs say so and never use the words of cryptography.
- **Packaged:** `roci package macos` carries `vault.key` and `vault.bin` in the bundle and seeds them into the app's folder (`rt.seed_home`), so the key travels with the program, as said above; the other systems wait for their packaging.

### 5.18 Scenes (decided 2026-11-17)

A sketch's phases (a title, the play, a pause, the end) are a state machine, and flags checked by every handler are the tangle Nystrom's Game Programming Patterns describes: a typo in `mode is "plya"` never matches and nothing warns, and `paused` can contradict `mode`. A **scene** is a named block that holds its own `draw`, `on`, `every` and `ui` blocks, plus `on enter` and `on leave`. A thing's own state (an enemy entering, home or diving) stays a text field and an `if`: no objects, no per-entity machines (§5.2).

```
draw [ background 20  sky ]          // outside every scene: runs in all of them, first

scene "title" [ draw [ titlescreen ]  on press "fire" [ go "play" ] ]
scene "play" [ on enter [ restart ]  draw [ advance  drawplay ]  on press "pause" [ visit "paused" ] ]
scene "paused" [ draw [ pausetext ]  on press "pause" [ return ] ]
```

- **`scene "name" [ ... ]`** is a statement at the left edge (`Scene_Not_Top` inside a block, and a scene cannot hold a scene), a `draw`, `on`, `every` or `ui` block each and nothing else (`Scene_Body`), with a name of its own (`Scene_Twice`). `scene` followed by a dot is the value instead (`scene.name`, `scene.time`, `scene.frame`: how long the scene has run, which stops while one is visited over it). Scenes may be in files other than `sketch.roci` (they are definitions until entered); a sketch with none behaves as before.
- **`go "name"`, `visit "name"` and `return`** are verbs that only ask; the change is made when every window has finished its frame (`roci.set_end_hook`), so a change never happens halfway through a frame and the code after a `go` still runs. The first ask of a frame stands. `go` ends every scene that is running, top first (`on leave`, tweens dropped), and starts the new one (`on enter`, its `every` timers counting from then); `visit` stops the scene under it and starts the new one on top; `return` ends the top one and the one under goes on, without its `on enter` again (`Return_Nothing` at run time when nothing was visited). `on enter` and `on leave` written outside every scene run for each scene. At most 8 changes are made in one end of frame (an `on enter` may ask for another).
- **Names are checked before the sketch runs** (`Scene_Unknown`, with the nearest name; `Scene_Name_Not_Text` for a name worked out by the sketch; `Go_In_Leave` for any of the three inside an `on leave`, which would loop). The first pass of `check` records the scene names (`check_scene.odin`), so a `go` written above its scene is matched too. The name is a literal only, as in §5.17.
- **What a scene owns.** Handlers, `every` timers, tweens and the `draw` and `ui` blocks written inside it carry its place (`Handler.scene`, `Tween.scene`, `Scene_Slot`) and take part only while it is current (blocks outside every scene always do). A `go` drops the tweens it started; a `visit` makes them, its timers and its clock wait and moves them on by the time waited at `return`. Variables stay global, and `on enter` is where a scene sets them up. Sound does not belong to a scene: music goes on under a pause.
- **A scene visited over stays on the screen** as its last finished frame (`roci.hold_frames`, `gfx.hold` and `begin_frame_from`: the frame's command arrays are cut back to what they held each frame, so what the scene on top draws is drawn over it and does not pile up), and while one is held the blocks written outside every scene are not run again (their picture is already there). Text input, widgets of the scene underneath and 3D items are cut the same way; the widgets of a scene underneath are not made.
- **Reload keeps the stack** by scene name, not place (the text may have added or moved scenes), and starts again in the first scene when the one it was in is gone (said in a notice). A restart (`Ctrl+R`, or a change to a line that opens a window) starts in the first scene.
- **Levels stay data, not scenes**: stage 3 is the play scene with `stage = 3`. Not offered: guards on transitions, parallel regions, nested scenes (Harel statecharts); `on return` (run when a visited scene comes back) is the one extra to reconsider once sketches ask. Not done yet: a scene's name in the identity of its widgets (a label in two scenes shares one remembered value), and compiled sketches (Phase 7 maps scenes to an Odin enum and a switch, so both back ends behave alike).
- **The C API has no scene functions**: a scene is a state of the program, and C keeps its own (`ABI_LANGUAGE` says so).
- **Words**: `go`, `visit` and `return` are verbs and so cannot be names (`examples/swarm` named a variable `go`); `scene` is a built-in value; `enter` and `leave` are events, not reserved.

### 5.19 Data files (decided 2026-11-17)

`load` reads a JSON, CSV, TSV, TOML or text file from `assets/data` (found by name, like a picture), and `save` writes one; `lines` and `files` are the two small verbs around them.

```
birds = load "birds.json"        // a record: birds.title, and birds.birds, a list of records
rows = load "scores.csv"         // a list of records, a field for each column of the first line
for each r in rows [ total = total + r.points ]
save summary "summary.json"      // goes where pictures do: output/ beside the sketch
for each verse in lines (load "poem.txt") [ ... ]
for each name in files in "images" [ ... ]
```

- **The shape comes from the file, at check time** (§5.3): the checker reads the file through a reader the caller installs (`lang.set_data_reader`; `roci run` and `roci check` install `folder.data_reader`, which looks in the sketch's `assets/`), so `birds.birds` and its fields are known before the sketch runs and a field the file lacks is `Unknown_Field`. An object is a record of its members; the objects of one place in a file (the items of a list) share one record shape whose fields are the members of all of them, in the order first seen (a missing member starts as the zero of its place); a number, text or true or false is that kind; `null` takes the kind of what else is in its place; a list of mixed kinds is a list of mixed values; a member that is a number in one object and text in another keeps the first kind, and the other is the empty value. A shape's place is the call and the path in the file (`data_sites`), so both passes of the checker make one shape. With no reader (a sketch checked alone) a data file is a mixed value. A name that is worked out while the sketch runs is not read by the checker and stays a picture, as `load` always did.
- **Names of fields**: a key or a column title becomes a name a sketch can write after a dot (`data.field_name`): "First Name" is `first_name`, "2nd" is `_2nd`. Two columns that come out the same are numbered (`size`, `size_2`).
- **Kinds**: JSON as it is (a number is a number, an object a record, a list a list); CSV and TSV have the names of the columns on the first line, a column of only numbers (an empty cell is 0) holds numbers and any other holds text, quotes follow RFC 4180, a line with more cells than the first is a problem and one with fewer has the rest empty; TOML is one record (the keys before any header, then each `[table]` as a field); a text file is one text (use `lines` to split it, with no `\r` and no empty last line).
- **Messages**: `Data_File_Missing` (also when the name is ambiguous) and `Data_File_Unreadable` (with the line and the reason), found at check time, so a missing or broken file is said once, with the sketch left to carry on; at run time the same messages say a file that went away or was broken by an edit, and the old value stays.
- **What `load` gives is kept**: the same list or record each time the call runs, read again when the file changes while the sketch is watched (`Data_Entry`, once a frame at most), so editing a data file changes a running sketch. It lives as long as the sketch.
- **`save value "name.ext"`** writes a value in the kind of file the name says: a record is an object, a list a list (a list of records is a table, with the names of its fields first, and a field a record lacks is an empty cell), a vector a list of its numbers, a color its `#rrggbbaa` text, a table an object; a window, picture or sound is null. A text file is written from a text, or from a list of texts one to a line; TOML from a record. It goes where `save` of a picture goes (`output/` of the sketch, or an absolute path) and is written at once beside its name and moved over it (`roci.write_text_file`). A value that holds itself, or a kind the file cannot hold, is `Save_Failed`, and the sketch goes on.
- **`save picture "name.png"`** (decided 2026-11-20, #57): a picture or a canvas as the first thing makes `save` write that picture as a PNG at its own size, at once (`roci.save_image`, `roci_save_picture` in C, `rt.save_picture_file` compiled), where the one-name form takes the window after the frame. A canvas is read back first, which stalls the frame; a name that does not end in `.png` is `Save_Failed`; a run of `#` is the frame number as for the window.
- **Where files may be**: `load` reads only `assets/` (and, with the dialogs, paths a player chose); `save` follows the rule pictures always had, which allows an absolute path as well.
- **C has the files, not the values**: `roci_read_file`, `roci_write_file`, `roci_file_count` and `roci_file_name` give and take text, since C has no record to parse into; `lines` is C's own string handling.
- **Words**: `lines` and `files` are verbs now (`examples/data` calls its loop name `verse`, because `line` is a verb too).
- **Spreadsheets and documents** (2026-11-17, `roci/data/zip.odin`, `xml.odin`, `office.odin`): `load "stock.xlsx"` and `"stock.ods"` read a sheet as a table (the first, or the one `sheet "Notes"` names: `load "stock.xlsx" sheet "Notes"`, a literal so the checker can read it), exactly as a CSV is (`table_from_rows`: the first row names the columns, a column of numbers is numbers), and `load "letter.odt"` is the document's text, a paragraph or heading to a line (`text:s`, `text:tab` and `text:line-break` as the spaces, tab and break they stand for; notes, bookmarks and comments left out). A cell is what it shows: a shared or inline text, a number as written, a truth as `true` or `false` (LibreOffice writes truths into Excel and OpenDocument files as 1 and 0 with a format, which are then numbers), a formula its last result, a date the number Excel keeps it as (OpenDocument says the date, which is text), an error empty. Rows with nothing in them are left out. They are zips of XML: our own zip reader (stored and deflated entries from the central directory, each checked against its stored CRC, no zip64, passwords or splitting, `MAX_UNPACKED` 256 MB so a bomb is a problem and not a crash) over `core:compress/deflate`, and our own XML reader (`core:encoding/xml` trims the spaces at the ends of a text, which paragraphs depend on; ours keeps them, reads with a stack, and refuses nesting past 512 or a file that is not well formed) and a limit of five million cells. Nothing writes these files (`write` says so, and suggests a CSV, which they open); `.docx` is not read yet. The fixtures in `tests/office` were made by LibreOffice from `book.fods` and `letter.html`. The C API has no spreadsheet function: a program that wants one reads a CSV.
- **Dialogs, `read`, `open` and the clipboard** (2026-11-17, `roci/platform/dialogs.odin`, `roci/abi_dialogs.odin`, `roci/interp/dialogs.odin`): `ask file "png jpg" [ ... ]`, `ask folder [ ... ]` and `ask save "name.json" [ ... ]` are verbs with a block, as `draw into` is, and nothing suspends (the decision above): the call shows SDL's dialog over the current window and keeps the block with the request's number, and the frame hook looks once a frame for answers (`run_asks`), running the block of an answered one with the built-in text `chosen` the path (empty anywhere else). A dialog closed without a choice runs nothing. At most four are open at once (`MAX_OPEN_DIALOGS`); a call past that is dropped, so a sketch that asks every frame does not pile them up. SDL calls back from the thread the system's dialog ran on, so the answers sit in a locked table, each taken by its own number (`dialog_new`, `dialog_deliver`, `dialog_take`): the tests play the player with `dialog_deliver`, and a headless sketch shows no dialog and waits for such an answer. The text before the block lists the kinds of file by their endings; `ask save` takes the name the dialog starts with and its ending is the kind. **A path chosen is remembered** (`Interp.allowed`), and it is the only path outside `assets/` that `load` (as a picture: a name worked out while the sketch runs is a picture), `read` and `open` take, so a downloaded sketch cannot look through a computer's files; `save` already took any absolute path. `read "name"` gives the text of any file (of `assets/`, found by name as a picture is, or a chosen path) and says `File_Not_Read` when it cannot; a data file chosen in a dialog is read as text, since the checker cannot know its shape (`load` of a computed name is a picture). `open "address"` hands a web address (`://`, `mailto:`, `tel:`), a chosen path or a file of `assets/` (as a `file://` address) to SDL's `OpenURL` and says `Open_Failed` when it cannot, which a headless sketch always does. `clipboard` is a built-in text (empty when there is none) and `copy to clipboard` the verb that sets it; a headless sketch has a clipboard of its own in memory. The words `chosen`, `clipboard`, `read` and `open` became reserved: `examples/garden`, `sengen` and `swarm` renamed a variable `chosen` to `picked`. C has `roci_ask` (kind `"file"`, `"folder"` or `"save"`, types, place) and `roci_answer` (NULL while open, empty when closed, else the path; answered once), `roci_clipboard`, `roci_set_clipboard` and `roci_open`. Unverified: the dialogs, the clipboard and `open` on every system (this Mac's SDL dialogs were not run, as the tests have no window), and on the phones, where SDL's dialogs may not exist (Phase 8).
- **`fetch`, `fetch picture` and `post`** (2026-11-18, `roci/platform/fetch.odin`, `roci/fetch.odin`, `roci/abi_fetch.odin`, `roci/interp/fetch.odin`, `vendor/curl`, `tools/net-libs.sh`): `fetch "url" [ ... ]` asks for an address on a thread of its own and runs its block when the answer has come, with the record `reply` (`status`, `ok`, `text`, `problem`, `picture`); `fetch picture` decodes what came as a picture; `post "url" "text" [ ... ]` sends a text; `header "line"` after the block adds a header line. Like the dialogs, the asking gives a number and a locked table holds the answers (`fetch_take`, `fetch_forget`; the table is the program's, never emptied whole, because sketches on several threads share it); the frame hook looks once a frame (`run_fetches`) and at most eight are open. The reply is cut at 64 MB, a request gives up after 30 seconds (10 to connect), up to five redirects are followed, and only http and https are allowed (`CURLOPT_PROTOCOLS_STR`, also for redirects). A headless run fetches nothing, so frames repeat; tests answer the requests with `platform.fetch_deliver`. **The library**: libcurl 8.23.0 (the curl licence) with mbedTLS 4.3.0 (Apache-2.0), built static with HTTP, HTTPS and web sockets only by `tools/net-libs.sh` into `.cache/net` (sources fetched and checked by `tools/fetch-net.sh`; `ci` and `docs` build them first, `odin run build -- net-libs` by hand; cmake and a C compiler are needed), linked through our copy of Odin's `vendor:curl` bindings in `vendor/curl`; nothing needs to be installed to run. Windows is built to use the system's Schannel (the script is written, not yet run there). The certificates are the computer's own (`/etc/ssl/cert.pem` and the other usual files, or Android's `/system/etc/security/cacerts`), found at run time; iOS has no such file, so its builds carry Mozilla's list (curl.se's `cacert.pem` of 2026-11-05, 118 roots, MPL-2.0 data kept unchanged in `vendor/curl`, embedded with `#load` for the iPhone subtargets only and given to libcurl from memory as `CAINFO_BLOB`; chosen by Fabrizio 2026-11-18 over verifying through Apple's trust store; it must be refreshed from curl.se with each release, ignores certificates a user installed on the phone, and `roci package` must ship its MPL-2.0 source notice with an iOS app; checked on the Mac that it accepts real sites and refuses expired and self-signed ones). The phones' libraries are built by `tools/net-libs.sh ios` and `android` (called by `spikes/mobile/ios/libs.sh` and `spikes/mobile/android/libs.sh`, which copy them into the phones' lib folders): checked 2026-11-18 only that they build, are arm64 for the right platform (iOS 15, Android API 28) and link into a program with Xcode and the NDK; fetching on a phone itself is not tried (Phase 8). HTTP/2, compression, cookies and proxies from the environment other than curl's own are not built or set. C has `roci_fetch`, `roci_fetch_done`, `roci_fetch_status`, `roci_fetch_text`, `roci_fetch_problem`, `roci_fetch_picture` and `roci_fetch_forget`. JSON from `reply.text` into a record waits (the checker cannot know its shape before the sketch runs).
- **`udp send` and `on udp`** (2026-11-18, `roci/udp.odin`, `roci/abi_udp.odin`, `roci/interp/udp.odin`): `on udp 9200 [ ... ]` runs for each datagram that arrives on that port and `udp send "text" to "host" port n` (or a list of bytes) sends one. `packet` is the datagram inside the block: `text`, `bytes` (numbers 0 to 255), `from` and `port` (checker site -9, `Packet_Outside_Event` outside). The sockets are OSC's (`platform/osc.odin`, whose packet now carries the sender's port): one listening socket and thread per port, at most 16 ports (`UDP_MAX_PORTS`), and one sending socket; the datagrams are taken once a frame and logged as `.Udp` happenings against the window that has the focus, matched to a handler by the port (`event_arg_matches`). `port` is required in `udp send` (UDP has no usual port); the largest datagram is 65507 bytes; mistakes are the runtime messages `Udp_Port_Busy`, `Udp_Host_Unknown` and `Udp_Data_Bad`, said once, and the sketch goes on. A headless run listens and sends nothing: `feed_udp` and `udp_sent_messages` are the tests' network. C has `roci_udp_send`, `roci_udp_listen` and `roci_on` with "udp" and the port as the argument (event kind 20, `roci_event.udp`). There is no `udp listen` verb, since the block names the port; replying to the sender, and a socket the sketch holds open to talk back, wait for `tcp`'s connections.
- **`tcp connect`, `tcp listen`, `tcp send`, `tcp close` and `on tcp`** (2026-11-18, `roci/tcp.odin`, `roci/platform/tcp.odin`, `roci/abi_tcp.odin`, `roci/interp/tcp.odin`): connections are named by a text label the sketch chooses (no new value kind; labels are checked at run time, as OSC addresses are), `tcp connect "chat" to "host" port n` and `tcp listen "server" port n` start them and `on tcp "chat"` (a line arrived), `on tcp open "chat"` and `on tcp close "chat"` (with the reason in `received.text`) hear them. `received` is the record `text`, `from`, `client` (checker site -10, `Received_Outside_Event` outside). What goes over a connection is lines: text up to a newline with a carriage return before it left off, `tcp send` of a text adds the newline, a list of numbers goes as exactly its bytes. One thread per connection (a read with a 20 ms timeout, so the same thread sends what waits and notices it was told to stop) and one to dial or to accept; the sketch never waits, and a client let go of while its thread is still dialing (which `core:net` cannot call off) is cleaned up by that thread (`dialing` and `abandoned` under the endpoint's lock). A server keeps at most 16 clients (`TCP_MAX_CLIENTS`, numbered from 1, `to n` answers one), a sketch has at most 8 connections and listeners (`TCP_MAX_ENDPOINTS`), a label is up to 64 letters, a line up to 1 MB. The same label to the same place again changes nothing (a reload), to another place replaces it. A client that ended is let go of when its last happening is taken (`tcp_step` reads whether it was over before it takes what it did). Runtime messages: `Tcp_Port_Busy`, `Tcp_Host_Unknown`, `Tcp_Unknown_Label`, `Tcp_Refused`. A headless run opens nothing: `feed_tcp`, `tcp_sent_messages` and `tcp_asked_for` are the tests' network. C has `roci_tcp_connect`, `roci_tcp_listen`, `roci_tcp_send`, `roci_tcp_close` and events of kinds 21 to 23 (`roci_event.tcp`). Not done: bytes without lines on the receiving side, TLS (`wss` waits for `websocket`, which libcurl does), and a connection timeout (a host that does not answer is waited for by its thread as long as the system waits, and shutting the sketch down does not wait for it).
- **`websocket connect`, `websocket send`, `websocket close` and `on websocket`** (2026-11-18, `roci/websocket.odin`, `roci/platform/websocket.odin`, `roci/abi_websocket.odin`, `roci/interp/websocket.odin`): the same shape as `tcp`, with a web socket named by a label: `websocket connect "feed" to "wss://..."`, `on websocket open "feed"`, `on websocket "feed"` (a message), `on websocket close "feed"`, and the same record `received` (`text`, `from` the host, `client` always 1). libcurl does the handshake, framing and TLS (`CURLOPT_CONNECT_ONLY` 2 and `curl_ws_recv` and `curl_ws_send`, protocols restricted to ws and wss, the same trusted certificates as `fetch`: `curl_trust` and `curl_start` in `platform/fetch.odin` are shared); a thread per socket loops a read (non-blocking, sleeping 5 ms when idle), sends what waits and notices it was told to stop, and a progress callback ends a connect that is still going when the sketch lets go of it, so closing never waits for a handshake. A message is the frames up to the last (`CURLWS_CONT` clear and nothing left), at most 8 MB; text and binary both reach the sketch as text, and `websocket send` of a list of numbers goes as a binary message; pings are answered by libcurl. At most 8 sockets (`WEBSOCKET_MAX`), a label up to 64 letters, the same label to the same address again changes nothing (a reload). Runtime messages `Websocket_Address_Bad`, `Websocket_Unknown_Label` and `Websocket_Refused`. The happenings are `.Ws_Open`, `.Ws_Message` and `.Ws_Close` in `Happened.tcp`, as a TCP connection's are; a headless run opens nothing (`feed_websocket`, `websocket_sent_messages`, `websocket_asked_for`). C has `roci_websocket_connect`, `roci_websocket_send`, `roci_websocket_close` and events of kinds 24 to 26. Checked by hand on the Mac against a local server over `ws://` and a public echo server over `wss://`; the phones and Windows are not tried (libcurl's WebSocket code is in the phone libraries). Not done: a web socket server, extra headers or sub-protocols, and a reconnect when one drops.
- **Still to come**: XML waits for someone to ask. Phase 6's second item (files, data, dialogs, network) is otherwise complete.

---

## 6. Feature parity map

Goal: everything the core and standard add-ons of Processing, openFrameworks (0.12.1) and Cinder do, in rocinante words. The rows below were verified on 2026-10-24 against the official references (see "Verification" after the table); rows are completed phase by phase, and updated here, in place.

| Area | Processing | openFrameworks | Cinder | rocinante (draft verbs) | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Window, app loop | `size`, `fullScreen`, `windowMove`, `windowTitle`, `windowResizable`, `frameRate`, `settings`, `loop`/`noLoop`, `redraw`, `exit` | `ofAppRunner`, `ofBaseApp`, `ofMainLoop`, multi-window via `ofCreateWindow` | App, Window | `window` (a window can be resized and moved after it opens), `title`, `fullscreen`, `fps` (the rate, and `window ... fps 60` to hold it there), `vsync`, `draw`, `pause`, `resume`, `quit`; window events `on resize`, `on move`, `on focus`; `fixed step` for a fixed time step (§4.7, §9) | 1 |
| Display | `displayWidth/Height`, `displayDensity`, `pixelDensity`, `cursor`, `noCursor`, `smooth` | screen size, `ofHideCursor` | Display | automatic HiDPI, `screens` (every connected display, with `name`, `pos`, `width`, `height`, `density`, `refresh` and `main`; built 2026-11-19) and `platform` (the system's name, built 2026-11-19), `safe` (the strips of a phone's screen that it keeps for itself, `top`, `bottom`, `left` and `right`, built 2026-11-20), `cursor` (a window setting: `window ... cursor none`, `set w cursor arrow`; more shapes such as `hand` and `crosshair` later), `smooth` (on by default; `no smooth` for pixel art), `orientation` on mobile | 1 |
| 2D primitives | `arc`, `circle`, `ellipse`, `line`, `point`, `quad`, `rect`, `square`, `triangle` | ofGraphics draw functions | 2D Graphics | same names | 1 |
| Style | `fill`, `noFill`, `stroke`, `noStroke`, `strokeWeight`, `strokeCap`, `strokeJoin`, `ellipseMode`, `rectMode`, `push`/`pop` | `ofSetColor`, `ofFill`, `ofSetLineWidth` | gl::color | `fill`, `no fill`, `stroke`, `no stroke`, `thickness`, `alpha`, `caps`, `joins`; per-element style words; `with … [ ]` and `push [ ]` scopes; named styles as records; gradients (`fill [ red teal ] angle 90`, `as radial`, `as conic`) (§5.9) | 1 |
| Color | `color`, `colorMode`, `lerpColor`, `red`/`green`/`blue`/`alpha`, `hue`/`saturation`/`brightness`, `background`, `clear` | `ofColor` | Color | CSS names (`red`), hex (`#ff8800`), grey number, `rgb`, `hsb`, `oklch`, `alpha`, `mix`, `background`, `clear`; channels 0–255 and alpha 0–100 (§5.9). Because `red`, `green` and `blue` are colour names, a colour's channels are read as fields (`c.r`, `c.g`, `c.b`, `c.a`) and `hue c`, `saturation c`, `brightness c` are verbs | 1 |
| Transform | `translate`, `rotate`, `rotateX/Y/Z`, `scale`, `shearX/Y`, `applyMatrix`, `pushMatrix`/`popMatrix`, `resetMatrix` | `ofTranslate`, `ofRotate`, `ofScale`, `ofNode` | gl::translate etc. | `move`, `turn`, `grow`, `shear`, block-scoped with `push [ ]` | 1 |
| Shapes and curves | `beginShape`/`vertex`/`endShape`, contours, `bezier`, `bezierVertex`, `curve`, `curveVertex`, `quadraticVertex`, detail/tightness, `PShape`, `createShape` | `ofPath`, `ofPolyline` | Path2d, Shape2d | `shape [ ]`, `vertex`, `bezier`, `curve`, `hole [ ]`, `path`, `polyline` with resample/smooth/simplify; `point on` a curve or bezier at a position along it, and its direction there (Processing's `bezierPoint`/`bezierTangent`) | 1 to 3 |
| Turtle | (none) | (none) | (none) | `forward`, `back`, `left`, `right`, `pen up/down`, `home`, the turtle in space (`turtle space`, `up`, `down`, `roll`; done 2026-11-13, §4.3) | 2 |
| Math | `map`, `lerp`, `constrain`, `dist`, `mag`, `norm`, trig, `degrees`/`radians`, `PVector`, constants `PI`/`TAU` | ofMath, glm | Vec, Matrix | `map`, `mix`, `clamp`, `dist`, `{ x, y }` vector literals, the usual number verbs (`abs`, `ceil`, `floor`, `round`, `sqrt`, `pow`, `exp`, `log`, `min`, `max`, `sign`, `fract`), trig, `pi`, `tau`; vector verbs `normalize` and `limit` (done 2026-11-12, for vectors of two to four numbers), `atan` (done 2026-11-12: `atan y x` and `atan v`, the angle in degrees from -180 to 180 clockwise from right, which is Processing's `atan2` and `PVector.heading`), and `dot`, `cross`, `rotate`, `angle between` still to come; quaternions in 3D (`slerp`) | 1 |
| Randomness, noise | `random`, `randomGaussian`, `randomSeed`, `noise`, `noiseSeed`, `noiseDetail` | `ofRandom`, `ofNoise` | Rand, Perlin | `random`, `random from … to …`, `anywhere`, `pick`, `gaussian`, `seed`, `noise` (Perlin, simplex, 1 to 4D), `fbm` | 1 |
| Time, animation | `millis`, `second`…`year`, `frameCount`, `delay` | `ofGetElapsedTimef`, `ofGetFrameNum` | Timeline, Tween, easing | `time`, `frame`, `clock`, `tween`, `ease`, `every` | 2 |
| Input | mouse vars and callbacks, `pmouseX`, keyboard vars and callbacks, `mouseWheel` | mouse, key, touch, drag-and-drop events | Mouse/Key/Touch events | `mouse`, `pmouse`, `key`, `touches`, `wheel`, `on …`, dropped files | 1 to 2 |

| Computer vision, depth | (OpenCV library) | ofxOpenCv, ofxKinect | (blocks) | extension libraries, after the first stable release | 9 |

### Verification (2026-10-24)

**What was read.** Processing: the whole reference index (processing.org/reference), every category. openFrameworks: the documentation index (openframeworks.cc/documentation), modules 3d, application, communication, events, gl, glm, graphics, math, sound and types. The `utils` and `video` modules, and the end of `types`, were cut off in the fetch, so those two are checked from knowledge of the library, not from the page; the page also does not state its version, so "0.12.1" is unverified. Cinder: the namespace list and class list (libcinder.org/docs), which have names but no descriptions.

**What the check changed.** The rows above now name the Processing index items that rocinante means to offer, which were missing before: list and text utilities, number formatting, `PVector` methods, curve points and tangents, window events, displays, `launch`, `noLights`, lighting falloff and specular, texturing. From openFrameworks: `ofDrawGrid`, `ofDrawAxis`, `ofDrawArrow`, vertical sync, orientation, clipboard, the fixed time step modes (`ofSetTimeModeFixedRate`). From Cinder: `Capsule`, multiple displays. It also found a clash: Processing's `red(c)`, `green(c)` and `blue(c)` cannot be verbs because `red`, `green` and `blue` are colour names; channels are fields instead.

**Not offered as such, by design.**
- Processing's Java language (classes, `extends`, types, `try`, `null`) and its stream and reader classes (`createInput`, `BufferedReader`, `PrintWriter`): rocinante has `load` and `save` and no types or exceptions (§5.2).
- `thread()` and openFrameworks' `ofThread`: users never see threads (§4.4); the core decides.
- `hint()` and openFrameworks' GL classes (`ofVbo`, `ofBufferObject`, renderers, window-settings classes): GPU details stay out of sight (non-negotiable 2); batching and instancing are automatic.
- Cinder's `cairo`, `qtime`, `dx` and `winrt` namespaces: `save "x.svg"`, `save "x.pdf"` and the platform video frameworks (§4.11) cover them.
- Cinder's `Arcball` and `CameraUi`: the default orbit camera (§4.3). Cinder's `Ray`, `AxisAlignedBox` and frustum types: the collision verbs (§5.13). Cinder's `Teapot`: not offered.

**Decided on the way** (2026-10-24): alignment modes become the origin words `as center` and `as corner` (§5.9), and Escape does not quit a sketch (`ofSetEscapeQuitsApp`), because it already leaves the widgets (§4.8).
